| | 300 |

|  | 300 |
|---|
|  | ACT |
|---|
|  | of 20 May 2005 |
|---|
|  | CRIMINAL CODE |
|---|
|  | The National Council of the Slovak Republic has adopted this Act: |
|---|
|  |
|
|---|
|  | PART ONE |
|---|
|  | GENERAL PART |
|---|
|

| § 1 |

|---|
|  | Subject of the Act |
|---|
|  | This Act regulates the fundamentals of criminal liability, types of punishments, types of protective measures, their imposition and the elements of criminal offences. |
|---|
|  | TITLE ONE |
|---|
|  | SCOPE OF THE ACT AND FUNDAMENTALS OF CRIMINAL LIABILITY |
|---|
|  | Division One |
|---|
|  | Criminal Code and its Scope |
|---|
|

| § 2 |

|---|
|  | Temporal Scope |
|---|
|  | (1) The criminality of an act shall be assessed and punishment shall be imposed according to the law in force at the time when the act was committed. If several laws enter into force between the commission of the act and the delivery of the judgment, the criminality of the act shall be assessed and punishment shall be imposed according to the law that is more favourable to the offender. |
|---|
|  | (2) The offender may be imposed such type of punishment as is permitted by the law in force at the time when the criminal offence is being decided, if it is more favourable to the offender. |
|---|
|  | (3) Unless this Act provides otherwise, a protective measure shall be imposed according to the law in force at the time when the protective measure is being decided. |
|---|
|

| § 3 |

|---|
|  | Territorial Scope |
|---|
|  | (1) The criminality of an act committed on the territory of the Slovak Republic shall be assessed according to this Act. |
|---|
|  | (2) A criminal offence shall be deemed to have been committed on the territory of the Slovak Republic even if the offender |
|---|
|  | a) committed the conduct at least in part on its territory, if the violation or endangerment of an interest protected by this Act occurred or was to occur wholly or in part outside its territory, or |
|---|
|  | b) committed the conduct outside the territory of the Slovak Republic, if the violation or endangerment of an interest protected by this Act was to occur here or if such consequence was to occur at least in part here. |
|---|
|  | (3) The criminality of an act committed outside the territory of the Slovak Republic on board a vessel sailing under the state flag of the Slovak Republic or on board an aircraft registered in the aircraft register of the Slovak Republic shall also be assessed according to this Act. |
|---|
|  | Personal Scope |
|---|
|

| § 4 |

|---|
|  | The criminality of an act committed outside the territory of the Slovak Republic by a citizen of the Slovak Republic or by a foreigner who has permanent residence on the territory of the Slovak Republic shall also be assessed according to this Act. |
|---|
|

| § 5 |

|---|
|  | The criminality of a particularly serious felony shall also be assessed according to this Act if the act was committed outside the territory of the Slovak Republic against a citizen of the Slovak Republic and the act is punishable at the place of commission or if the place of commission is not subject to any criminal jurisdiction. |
|---|
|

| § 5a |

|---|
|  | The criminality of unauthorised production of narcotic drugs and psychotropic substances, poisons or precursors, their possession and trafficking in them (§ 171 and 172), legalisation of proceeds from criminal activity (§ 233 to 234), counterfeiting, alteration and unauthorised production of money and securities (§ 270), uttering of counterfeit, altered and unauthorisedly produced money and securities (§ 271), production and possession of counterfeiting instruments (§ 272), counterfeiting, alteration and unauthorised production of revenue stamps, postal stamps, labels and postal stamps (§ 274), counterfeiting and alteration of control technical measures for marking goods (§ 275), establishment, conspiracy and support of a terrorist group or its member (§ 297), unauthorised production and possession of nuclear materials, radioactive substances, high-risk chemical substances and high-risk biological agents and toxins (§ 298 and 299), conspiracy against the Slovak Republic (§ 312), terror (§ 313 and 314), sabotage (§ 315 and 316), sabotage (§ 317), espionage (§ 318), attack on a public authority (§ 321), attack on a public official (§ 323), counterfeiting and alteration of a public document, official seal, official closure, official emblem and official mark (§ 352), endangerment of confidential facts and classified facts (§ 353), people smuggling (§ 355), endangerment of peace (§ 417), genocide (§ 418), terrorist attack (§ 419), certain forms of participation in terrorism (§ 419b), financing of terrorism (§ 419c), travel for the purpose of terrorism (§ 419d), inhumanity (§ 425), use of a prohibited means of combat and unlawful conduct of combat (§ 426), pillage in the area of military operations (§ 427), abuse of internationally recognised designations and state emblems (§ 428), war cruelty (§ 431), persecution of the population (§ 432), war lawlessness (§ 433) shall also be assessed according to this Act even if such criminal offence was committed outside the territory of the Slovak Republic by a foreigner who does not have permanent residence on the territory of the Slovak Republic. |
|---|
|

| § 6 |

|---|
|  | (1) The criminality of an act committed outside the territory of the Slovak Republic by a foreigner who does not have permanent residence on the territory of the Slovak Republic shall also be assessed according to this Act if |
|---|
|  | a) the act is also punishable under the law in force on the territory where it was committed, |
|---|
|  | b) the offender was detained or arrested on the territory of the Slovak Republic, and |
|---|
|  | c) he was not extradited for criminal prosecution to a foreign state. |
|---|
|  | (2) However, the offender referred to in paragraph 1 may not be imposed a punishment more severe than that provided by the law of the state on whose territory the criminal offence was committed. |
|---|
|

| § 7 |

|---|
|  | Scope According to International Treaties |
|---|
|  | (1) The criminality of an act shall be assessed according to this Act also if so provided by an international treaty that has been ratified and promulgated in the manner established by law, by which the Slovak Republic is bound. |
|---|
|  | (2) The provisions of § 3 to 6 shall not apply if not permitted by an international treaty that has been ratified and promulgated in the manner established by law, by which the Slovak Republic is bound. |
|---|
|

| § 7a |

|---|
|  | Scope for Imposition of Protective Measures |
|---|
|  | (1) A protective measure under this Act may be imposed if the criminality of an act in connection with the commission of which the protective measure is to be imposed is assessed according to it. |
|---|
|  | (2) The provision of paragraph 1 shall also apply if the offender of an otherwise punishable act is not criminally liable or if it concerns a person who cannot be prosecuted or convicted. |
|---|
|

| § 7b |

|---|
|  | Execution and Recognition of a Decision of Another State |
|---|
|  | (1) A decision of a court of another state in a criminal matter may be executed on the territory of the Slovak Republic or may have other legal effects only if so provided by an international treaty or by law. |
|---|
|  | (2) A final conviction by a court of another Member State of the European Union in criminal proceedings shall be taken into account for the purposes of criminal proceedings in the same way as if it had been issued by a court of the Slovak Republic, if it was issued for an act punishable also under the legal order of the Slovak Republic. |
|---|
|  | Division Two |
|---|
|  | Fundamentals of Criminal Liability |
|---|
|  | SECTION ONE |
|---|
|  | CONCEPT AND TYPES OF CRIMINAL OFFENCE |
|---|
|

| § 8 |

|---|
|  | Criminal Offence |
|---|
|  | A criminal offence is an unlawful act the elements of which are set out in this Act, unless this Act provides otherwise. |
|---|
|

| § 9 |

|---|
|  | Types of Criminal Offences |
|---|
|  | A criminal offence is a misdemeanour and a felony. |
|---|
|

| § 10 |

|---|
|  | Misdemeanour |
|---|
|  | (1) A misdemeanour is |
|---|
|  | a) a criminal offence committed by negligence, or |
|---|
|  | b) an intentional criminal offence for which this Act in the special part provides a punishment of imprisonment with an upper limit of the sentencing range not exceeding five years. |
|---|
|  | (2) It is not a misdemeanour if, having regard to the manner of execution of the act and its consequences, the circumstances under which the act was committed, the degree of culpability and the motive of the offender, its seriousness is negligible. |
|---|
|

| § 11 |

|---|
|  | Felony |
|---|
|  | (1) A felony is an intentional criminal offence for which this Act in its special part establishes a penalty of imprisonment with an upper limit of the sentence exceeding five years. |
|---|
|  | (2) A felony also exists where, in a more serious factual basis of a misdemeanour committed intentionally, an upper limit of the sentence exceeding five years is established. |
|---|
|  | (3) A felony for which this Act establishes a penalty of imprisonment with a lower limit of the sentence of at least ten years shall be considered particularly serious. |
|---|
|

| § 12 |

|---|
|  | Place of commission of a criminal offence |
|---|
|  | The place of commission of a criminal offence is any place where |
|---|
|  | a) the offender acted, or |
|---|
|  | b) the consequence envisaged by this Act occurred or, according to the offender's conception, was to occur. |
|---|
|

| § 13 |

|---|
|  | Preparation for a felony |
|---|
|  | (1) Preparation for a felony is conduct consisting in the intentional organisation of a felony, the procurement or adaptation of means or instruments for its commission, in conspiracy, assembly, instigation, solicitation or aiding such a felony, or in other intentional creation of conditions for its commission, where an attempt or completion of the felony has not occurred. |
|---|
|  | (2) Preparation for a felony is punishable according to the sentence established for the felony towards which it was directed. |
|---|
|  | (3) The punishability of preparation for a felony ceases if the offender voluntarily |
|---|
|  | a) desisted from further conduct directed towards the commission of the felony and removed the danger which arose to the interest protected by this Act from the undertaken preparation, or |
|---|
|  | b) made a notification of the preparation for the felony to an authority active in criminal proceedings or to the Police Force at a time when the danger which arose to the interest protected by this Act from the undertaken preparation could still be removed; a soldier may also make this notification to his superior or service authority, and a person serving a sentence of imprisonment or in custody may also make it to a member of the Prison and Court Guard Service. |
|---|
|  | (4) The provision of paragraph 3 does not affect the punishability of the offender for another criminal offence which he has already committed by such conduct. |
|---|
|

| § 14 |

|---|
|  | Attempt of a criminal offence |
|---|
|  | (1) An attempt of a criminal offence is conduct which directly aims at the completion of a criminal offence, which the offender committed with the intention of committing a criminal offence, where the completion of the criminal offence has not occurred. |
|---|
|  | (2) An attempt of a criminal offence is punishable according to the sentence established for the completed criminal offence. |
|---|
|  | (3) The punishability of an attempt of a criminal offence ceases if the offender voluntarily |
|---|
|  | a) desisted from further conduct necessary for the completion of the criminal offence and removed the danger which arose to the interest protected by this Act from the undertaken attempt, or |
|---|
|  | b) made a notification of the attempt of the criminal offence to an authority active in criminal proceedings or to the Police Force at a time when the danger which arose to the interest protected by this Act from the undertaken attempt could still be removed; a soldier may also make this notification to his superior or service authority, and a person serving a sentence of imprisonment or in custody may also make it to a member of the Prison and Court Guard Service. |
|---|
|  | (4) The provision of paragraph 3 does not affect the punishability of the offender for another criminal offence which he has already committed by such conduct. |
|---|
|  | Culpability |
|---|
|

| § 15 |

|---|
|  | A criminal offence is committed intentionally if the offender |
|---|
|  | a) intended to violate or endanger an interest protected by this Act in the manner specified in this Act, or |
|---|
|  | b) knew that by his conduct he could cause such violation or endangerment, and in the event that he caused it, he accepted it. |
|---|
|

| § 16 |

|---|
|  | A criminal offence is committed by negligence if the offender |
|---|
|  | a) knew that he could violate or endanger an interest protected by this Act in the manner specified in this Act, but without reasonable grounds relied that he would not cause such violation or endangerment, or |
|---|
|  | b) did not know that by his conduct he could cause such violation or endangerment, although in view of the circumstances and his personal conditions he should and could have known it. |
|---|
|

| § 17 |

|---|
|  | For the punishability of an act committed by a natural person, intentional culpability is required, unless this Act expressly provides that culpability by negligence is sufficient. |
|---|
|

| § 18 |

|---|
|  | An aggravating circumstance or a circumstance which conditions the application of a higher sentence shall be taken into account if it concerns |
|---|
|  | a) a more serious consequence, even where the offender caused it by negligence, unless this Act requires intentional culpability also in this case, or |
|---|
|  | b) another fact, even where the offender did not know of it, although in view of the circumstances and his personal conditions he should and could have known of it, unless this Act requires that the offender knew of it. |
|---|
|  | SECOND SECTION |
|---|
|  | PERPETRATOR, CO-PERPETRATOR AND PARTICIPANT IN A CRIMINAL OFFENCE |
|---|
|

| § 19 |

|---|
|  | Perpetrator |
|---|
|  | (1) The perpetrator of a criminal offence is the person who committed the criminal offence himself. |
|---|
|  | (2) The perpetrator of a criminal offence may be a natural person and a legal person under the conditions established by a special regulation. |
|---|
|

| § 20 |

|---|
|  | Co-perpetrator |
|---|
|  | Where a criminal offence was committed by the joint conduct of two or more perpetrators (co-perpetrators), each of them is liable as if he had committed the criminal offence himself. |
|---|
|

| § 21 |

|---|
|  | Participant |
|---|
|  | (1) A participant in a completed criminal offence or in its attempt is the person who intentionally |
|---|
|  | a) devised or directed the commission of the criminal offence (organiser), |
|---|
|  | b) induced another to commit the criminal offence (instigator), |
|---|
|  | c) requested another to commit the criminal offence (solicitor), or |
|---|
|  | d) provided assistance to another for the commission of the criminal offence, in particular by procuring means, removing obstacles, giving advice, strengthening the resolve, promising to assist after the criminal offence (aider). |
|---|
|  | (2) The provisions on the criminal liability of the perpetrator shall apply to the criminal liability of the participant, unless this Act provides otherwise. |
|---|
|  | THIRD SECTION |
|---|
|  | CIRCUMSTANCES EXCLUDING CRIMINAL LIABILITY |
|---|
|

| § 22 |

|---|
|  | Age |
|---|
|  | (1) A person who at the time of committing an act otherwise criminal had not reached the age of fourteen years is not criminally liable. |
|---|
|  | (2) For the criminal offence of sexual abuse under § 201, a person who at the time of committing the act had not reached the age of fifteen years is not criminally liable. |
|---|
|

| § 23 |

|---|
|  | Insanity |
|---|
|  | A person who, due to a mental disorder at the time of committing an act otherwise criminal, could not recognise its unlawfulness or control his conduct, is not criminally liable for this act, unless this Act provides otherwise. |
|---|
|  | FOURTH SECTION |
|---|
|  | CIRCUMSTANCES EXCLUDING THE UNLAWFULNESS OF AN ACT |
|---|
|

| § 24 |

|---|
|  | Necessity |
|---|
|  | (1) An act otherwise criminal by which someone averts a danger directly threatening an interest protected by this Act is not a criminal offence. |
|---|
|  | (2) Necessity does not exist if it was possible to avert the danger directly threatening the interest protected by this Act under the given circumstances in another way, or if the consequence caused is manifestly more serious than the one which was threatened. Likewise, necessity does not exist if the person to whom the danger directly threatened was obliged under a generally binding legal regulation to endure it. |
|---|
|

| § 25 |

|---|
|  | Necessary Defence |
|---|
|  | (1) An otherwise criminal act by which someone repels a directly threatening or continuing attack on an interest protected by this Act is not a criminal offence. |
|---|
|  | (2) It is not necessary defence if the defence was quite obviously disproportionate to the attack, in particular to its manner, place and time, circumstances relating to the person of the attacker or to the person of the defender. |
|---|
|  | (3) A person who repels an attack in the manner referred to in paragraph 2 shall not be criminally liable if he acted in strong agitation caused by the attack, in particular as a result of confusion, fear or fright. |
|---|
|  | (4) If someone, having regard to the circumstances of the case, mistakenly believes that an attack is threatening, this does not exclude criminal liability for an act committed through negligence if the mistake is based on negligence. |
|---|
|

| § 26 |

|---|
|  | Authorised Use of a Weapon |
|---|
|  | (1) The use of a weapon in accordance with the law is not a criminal offence. |
|---|
|  | (2) The use of a weapon in accordance with the law shall also be deemed to include its use against another person in one's dwelling for the protection of life, health or property, if the person unlawfully enters the dwelling or unlawfully remains in it and it is not a case of necessary defence. This does not apply if death was intentionally caused to another person in the process. |
|---|
|

| § 27 |

|---|
|  | Permissible Risk |
|---|
|  | (1) An otherwise criminal act is not a criminal offence if someone, in accordance with the achieved state of knowledge, carries out a socially beneficial activity in the field of production and research, if the socially beneficial result expected from carrying out the act cannot be achieved without the risk of endangering an interest protected by this Act. |
|---|
|  | (2) It is not a permissible risk if the result to which the act is directed quite obviously does not correspond to the degree of risk or if carrying out the act is contrary to a generally binding legal regulation, the public interest, the principles of humanity or is contrary to good morals. |
|---|
|

| § 28 |

|---|
|  | Exercise of a Right and Duty |
|---|
|  | (1) An otherwise criminal act is not a criminal offence if it involves the exercise of a right or duty arising from a generally binding legal regulation, from a decision of a court or other public authority, from the performance of work or other tasks or from a contract that does not contradict a generally binding legal regulation or circumvent it; the manner of exercising rights and duties must not contradict a generally binding legal regulation. |
|---|
|  | (2) The provision of paragraph 1 shall not apply if the criminal offence of genocide under § 418, the criminal offence of enforced disappearance under § 420a or the criminal offence of inhumanity under § 425 was committed by complying with an order, instruction, command or directive of an executive authority or a superior. |
|---|
|  | (3) The provision of paragraph 1 shall not apply if the criminal offence of war crimes under § 433 was committed by complying with an order, instruction, command or directive of an executive authority or a superior, except where the person who complied with such order, instruction, command or directive, |
|---|
|  | a) had a legal duty to comply with such order, instruction, command or directive, |
|---|
|  | b) did not know that such order, instruction, command or directive was unlawful, and |
|---|
|  | c) the content of such order, instruction, command or directive did not indicate that it was unlawful. |
|---|
|

| § 29 |

|---|
|  | Consent of the Injured Party |
|---|
|  | (1) An otherwise criminal act is not a criminal offence if it was carried out with the consent of the injured party and is not directed against his life or health. |
|---|
|  | (2) It is not consent of the injured party if the consent was not given in advance, was not serious and voluntary or if another criminal offence was committed in connection with it. |
|---|
|  | (3) The provision of paragraph 1 shall not apply if, according to the factual elements of the criminal offence, the act is to be criminal even when the consent of the injured party under paragraph 1 was given. |
|---|
|

| § 30 |

|---|
|  | Performance of the Task of an Agent |
|---|
|  | (1) An otherwise criminal act is not a criminal offence if an agent appointed under a special regulation, in detecting a criminal offence and in identifying its perpetrator, endangers or violates an interest protected by this Act only because he was forced to do so by a criminal group or terrorist group in which he operates, or if he commits such an act in justified fear for the life or health of himself or a close person. |
|---|
|  | (2) The provision of paragraph 1 does not apply if the agent commits the criminal offence of premeditated murder under § 144, murder under § 145, rape under § 199, sexual violence under § 200, sexual abuse under § 201, general endangerment under § 284 paragraphs 2 to 4, endangering the safety of an aircraft and ship under § 291, hijacking an aircraft abroad under § 293, treason under § 311, conspiracy against the Slovak Republic under § 312, terror under § 313, § 314, sabotage under § 315, § 316, sabotage under § 317, espionage under § 318, genocide under § 418, terrorist attack under § 419, certain forms of participation in terrorism under § 419b, financing of terrorism under § 419c, travel for the purpose of terrorism under § 419d or inhumanity under § 425 or if by the act referred to in paragraph 1 he causes serious injury to health or death. |
|---|
|  | (3) An otherwise criminal act referred to in § 332 to 334 and in § 336 paragraph 2 committed for the purpose of detecting a criminal offence or identifying the perpetrator of a criminal offence under § 326, § 328 to 330, § 336 paragraph 1, § 336a or § 336b in the manner established in the Criminal Procedure Code is not a criminal offence. |
|---|
|  | SECOND CHAPTER |
|---|
|  | SANCTIONS |
|---|
|  | Part One |
|---|
|  | Types of Sanctions |
|---|
|

| § 31 |

|---|
|  | Punishment and Protective Measure |
|---|
|  | (1) Sanctions under this Act are punishments and protective measures, which are the legal consequences of a committed criminal offence or otherwise criminal act. |
|---|
|  | (2) Punishment is a detriment to the personal freedom, property or other rights of the convicted person, which may be imposed on the perpetrator only by a court under this Act for a committed criminal offence. |
|---|
|  | (3) A protective measure is a detriment to the personal freedom or property of the convicted person or another person, which may be imposed only by a court under this Act in the interest of protecting society from criminal offences or otherwise criminal acts. |
|---|
|

| § 32 |

|---|
|  | Types of Punishments |
|---|
|  | For committed criminal offences, the court may impose on the perpetrator who is a natural person only |
|---|
|  | a) punishment of imprisonment, |
|---|
|  | b) punishment of house arrest, |
|---|
|  | c) punishment of community service, |
|---|
|  | d) monetary punishment, |
|---|
|  | e) punishment of forfeiture of property |
|---|
|  | f) punishment of forfeiture of a thing, |
|---|
|  | g) punishment of prohibition of activity, |
|---|
|  | h) punishment of prohibition of residence, |
|---|
|  | i) punishment of prohibition of attendance at public events, |
|---|
|  | j) punishment of loss of honorary titles and decorations, |
|---|
|  | k) punishment of loss of military and other rank, |
|---|
|  | l) punishment of expulsion. |
|---|
|

| § 33 |

|---|
|  | Types of Protective Measures |
|---|
|  | Protective measures are: |
|---|
|  | a) protective treatment, |
|---|
|  | b) protective education, |
|---|
|  | c) protective supervision, |
|---|
|  | d) detention, |
|---|
|  | e) confiscation of a thing, |
|---|
|  | f) confiscation of part of property. |
|---|
|  | Part Two |
|---|
|  | Basic Principles of Imposing Sanctions |
|---|
|

| § 34 |

|---|
|  | Principles of Imposing Punishments |
|---|
|  | (1) Punishment shall ensure the protection of society from the offender by preventing him from committing further criminal activity and creating conditions for his education so that he leads a proper life and at the same time deters others from committing criminal offences; punishment also expresses the moral condemnation of the offender by society. |
|---|
|  | (2) Only such type of punishment and only in such measure as is established in this Act may be imposed on the offender, while this Act in its special part establishes only the sentencing ranges for imprisonment. |
|---|
|  | (3) Punishment shall affect only the offender, so as to ensure the least possible impact on his family and persons close to him. |
|---|
|  | (4) In determining the type of punishment and its measure, the court shall take into account in particular the manner of committing the act and its consequence, culpability, motive, aggravating circumstances, mitigating circumstances and the person of the offender, his circumstances and the possibility of his reform. In determining the type of punishment and its measure, the court shall also take into account that the offender of a criminal offence obtained pecuniary gain from the criminal offence; if the property or personal circumstances of the offender do not prevent it or it will not be to the detriment of compensation for damage, it shall impose on him, taking into account the amount of this pecuniary gain, in addition to another punishment also some punishment by which it will affect him in property, unless it imposes such punishment on him as independent under the conditions according to paragraph 6. |
|---|
|  | (5) In determining the type of punishment and its measure, the court shall take into account |
|---|
|  | a) in the case of co-perpetrators also the extent to which the conduct of each of them contributed to the commission of the criminal offence, |
|---|
|  | b) in the case of an organiser, instigator, inciter and accomplice also the significance and nature of their participation in the committed criminal offence, |
|---|
|  | c) in the case of preparation for a felony and in the case of an attempt of a criminal offence also the extent to which the conduct of the offender approached the completion of the criminal offence, as well as the circumstances and reasons for which the completion of the criminal offence did not occur. |
|---|
|  | (6) The punishments referred to in § 32 may be imposed independently or several of these punishments may be imposed concurrently. For a criminal offence whose upper limit of the sentencing range for imprisonment established in the special part of the Act exceeds five years, the court must impose a sentence of imprisonment, unless this Act provides otherwise. |
|---|
|  | (7) The following punishments may not be imposed concurrently |
|---|
|  | a) imprisonment and house arrest, |
|---|
|  | b) imprisonment and community service, |
|---|
|  | c) fine and forfeiture of property, |
|---|
|  | d) forfeiture of a thing and forfeiture of property, |
|---|
|  | e) prohibition of residence and expulsion. |
|---|
|

| § 35 |

|---|
|  | Principles of Imposing Protective Measures |
|---|
|  | (1) A protective measure may be imposed on the offender of a criminal offence either in addition to punishment, or even when waiving the punishment of the offender, if the protective measure ensures the protection of society from the offender more effectively than punishment. |
|---|
|  | (2) A protective measure may affect a person only in such a way as to ensure the least possible impact on his family and persons close to him. |
|---|
|  | (3) A protective measure may also be imposed on the offender of an otherwise criminal act or on another person, if it is necessary to ensure the protection of society from the commission of new criminal offences. |
|---|
|  | (4) Protective supervision may not be imposed in addition to protective education. |
|---|
|  | (5) In imposing protective measures, the court is not guided by the principle of proportionality to the committed act, but by the need to protect society, while also taking into account the need for treatment, education or completion of the reform of the offender or another person. In imposing a protective measure, the court shall also take into account that the offender of a criminal offence or another person obtained pecuniary gain from the criminal offence; if the property or personal circumstances of the offender or another person do not prevent it, it shall impose on him, taking into account the amount of this pecuniary gain, some protective measure by which it will affect him in property. |
|---|
|  | (6) The execution of a protective measure must end at the latest upon achieving its purpose, or upon the expiry of the period for which it was imposed, or upon reaching the age of the convicted person or another person established by law. |
|---|
|  | Mitigating Circumstances and Aggravating Circumstances |
|---|
|

| § 36 |

|---|
|  | A mitigating circumstance is that the offender |
|---|
|  | a) committed the criminal offence in justifiable strong emotional disturbance, |
|---|
|  | b) committed the criminal offence as a result of lack of knowledge or experience, |
|---|
|  | c) committed the criminal offence in connection with the negative consequences of his illness, |
|---|
|  | d) committed the criminal offence at an age close to the age of juveniles or as a person of advanced age, if this fact had an impact on his intellectual or volitional capacity, |
|---|
|  | e) committed the criminal offence under the pressure of dependence or subordination, |
|---|
|  | f) committed the criminal offence under the influence of threat or coercion, |
|---|
|  | g) committed the criminal offence as a result of distress which he did not cause himself, |
|---|
|  | h) committed the criminal offence under the influence of distressing personal circumstances or family circumstances which he did not cause himself, |
|---|
|  | i) committed the criminal offence while averting an attack or other danger or acting in circumstances which, if further conditions were met, would otherwise exclude the criminal nature of the act, but acted without the conditions of necessary defence, extreme necessity, exercise of a right and duty or consent of the injured party, authorised use of a weapon, permitted risk or performance of the tasks of an agent being fully met, |
|---|
|  | j) led a proper life before committing the criminal offence, |
|---|
|  | k) contributed to the removal of the harmful consequences of the criminal offence or voluntarily compensated for the damage caused, |
|---|
|  | l) confessed to committing the criminal offence and sincerely regretted the criminal offence, |
|---|
|  | m) himself reported the criminal offence to the competent authorities, |
|---|
|  | n) assisted the competent authorities in clarifying criminal activity, or |
|---|
|  | o) contributed to the detection or conviction of an organised group, criminal group or terrorist group. |
|---|
|

| § 37 |

|---|
|  | An aggravating circumstance is that the offender |
|---|
|  | a) committed the criminal offence from a particularly reprehensible motive, |
|---|
|  | b) committed the criminal offence as retaliation against another for fulfilling an obligation arising from law or other generally binding legal regulation towards the offender, in particular against a pedagogical employee or professional employee, |
|---|
|  | c) committed the criminal offence in order to frustrate or make it difficult for another to exercise his fundamental rights and freedoms, or in order to facilitate or conceal another criminal offence, |
|---|
|  | d) committed the criminal offence during a natural disaster or other extraordinary event seriously threatening the life or health of people, other fundamental rights and freedoms, the constitutional order, property, public order or morality, |
|---|
|  | e) abused his employment, profession, function or position to achieve an unjustified or disproportionate advantage, |
|---|
|  | f) committed the criminal offence publicly, |
|---|
|  | g) committed the criminal offence in a place enjoying special protection according to a generally binding legal regulation, in particular in the house or apartment of another, |
|---|
|  | h) committed several criminal offences, |
|---|
|  | i) abused a person who is not criminally responsible for committing the criminal offence, |
|---|
|  | j) induced a juvenile to commit the criminal offence, |
|---|
|  | k) committed the criminal offence as an organiser, |
|---|
|  | l) committed the criminal offence in connection with a foreign power or foreign agent, |
|---|
|  | m) has already been convicted of a criminal offence; the court may, according to the nature of the previous conviction, disregard this circumstance, |
|---|
|  | n) committed the criminal offence as a member of a group of persons during movement to the venue of a public event or from the venue of a public event, or |
|---|
|  | o) committed the criminal offence on the grounds of affiliation to a sports club. |
|---|
|

| § 38 |

|---|
|  | (1) A circumstance which is a statutory element of a criminal offence may not be taken into account as a mitigating circumstance, an aggravating circumstance, a circumstance conditioning the imposition of a sentence below the statutory lower limit of the sentencing range, or a circumstance conditioning the application of a higher sentencing range. |
|---|
|  | (2) In determining the type of sentence and its measure, the court must take into account the ratio and degree of gravity of mitigating circumstances and aggravating circumstances. |
|---|
|  | (3) If the ratio of mitigating circumstances prevails, the upper limit of the statutory sentencing range shall be reduced by one third. |
|---|
|  | (4) If the ratio of aggravating circumstances prevails, the lower limit of the statutory sentencing range shall be increased by one third. |
|---|
|  | (5) In the case of repeated commission of a felony, the lower limit of the statutory sentencing range shall be increased by one half; in such case, the provision of paragraph 4 shall not apply. |
|---|
|  | (6) In the case of repeated commission of a particularly serious felony, the lower limit of the statutory sentencing range shall be increased by two thirds; in such case, the provisions of paragraphs 4 and 5 shall not apply. |
|---|
|  | (7) The provisions of paragraphs 4 to 6 shall not apply if an increased aggregate sentence or a cumulative sentence is imposed simultaneously pursuant to § 41(2) or pursuant to § 42, if the simultaneous application of these provisions would be disproportionately severe for the offender. |
|---|
|  | (8) The reduction of the upper limit or the increase of the lower limit of the sentencing range pursuant to paragraphs 3 to 6 shall be carried out only within the statutory sentencing range; the basis for the reduction or increase of the sentencing range is the difference between the upper and lower limits of the statutory sentencing range. The reduction of the upper limit or the increase of the lower limit of the statutory sentencing range shall not apply in cases where the special part of the Act provides only for a sentence of imprisonment for twenty-five years or life. |
|---|
|

| § 39 |

|---|
|  | Extraordinary reduction of sentence |
|---|
|  | (1) If the court, having regard to the circumstances of the case or to the circumstances of the offender, considers that the application of the sentencing range established by this Act would be disproportionately severe for the offender and that a sentence of shorter duration is sufficient to ensure the protection of society, it may impose a sentence below the lower limit of the sentence established by this Act. |
|---|
|  | (2) The court may reduce the sentence below the lower limit of the sentencing range established by this Act also when sentencing an offender |
|---|
|  | a) for preparation for a felony or for an attempt to commit a criminal offence and if, having regard to the nature and gravity of the preparation or attempt, the court considers that the application of the sentencing range established by this Act would be disproportionately severe for the offender and that a sentence of shorter duration is sufficient for the protection of society, |
|---|
|  | b) who has contributed to a significant extent to the clarification of a criminal offence committed for the benefit of a criminal group or a terrorist group or has helped to prevent the commission of a criminal offence which another person was preparing or attempting to commit for the benefit of a criminal group or a terrorist group by reporting its activity to the authorities active in criminal proceedings and providing them with information which they would not otherwise have obtained, and thereby helping them to prevent or mitigate the consequences of the criminal offence, to identify or convict the offenders or to secure evidence of the criminal offence for the purpose of convicting the criminal group or terrorist group, |
|---|
|  | c) who committed a criminal offence in a state of diminished responsibility, and the court considers that, having regard to the health condition of the offender, it would be possible, while simultaneously imposing protective treatment, to achieve the protection of society also by a sentence of shorter duration, and is not bound by the limitations referred to in paragraph 3 and simultaneously imposes protective treatment, |
|---|
|  | d) in proceedings on an agreement on the admission of guilt and acceptance of punishment, or |
|---|
|  | e) who has participated to a particularly significant extent in the clarification of a criminal offence of corruption under Chapter Eight of Part Three of the Special Part of this Act, a criminal offence of establishing, organising and supporting a criminal group under § 296, a criminal offence of establishing, organising and supporting a terrorist group under § 297 or a particularly serious felony committed by an organised group, a criminal group or a terrorist group, or in the identification or conviction of its offender by providing evidence of such an offence in criminal proceedings, if, having regard to the nature and gravity of the criminal offence committed by him, the court considers that the purpose of the sentence can be achieved also by a sentence of shorter duration; the reduction of a sentence of imprisonment below the lower limit of the sentencing range must not be applied to the organiser, instigator or procurer of the criminal offence about which he provided evidence in criminal proceedings. |
|---|
|  | (3) When imposing a sentence below the statutory sentencing range, however, the court may not impose |
|---|
|  | a) a sentence of imprisonment of less than twenty years if the offender is convicted of a criminal offence of premeditated murder under § 144(3), genocide under § 418(3), terrorist attack under § 419(2), inhumanity under § 425(2) or war crime under § 433(2), |
|---|
|  | b) a sentence of imprisonment of less than eight years if the lower limit of the sentencing range of imprisonment in the special part of this Act is at least fifteen years, |
|---|
|  | c) a sentence of imprisonment of less than five years if the lower limit of the sentencing range of imprisonment in the special part of this Act is at least ten years, |
|---|
|  | d) a sentence of imprisonment of less than two years if the lower limit of the sentencing range of imprisonment in the special part of this Act is at least five years, |
|---|
|  | e) a sentence of imprisonment of less than six months if the lower limit of the sentencing range of imprisonment in the special part of this Act is less than five years, and |
|---|
|  | f) a sentence of prohibition of activity, a sentence of prohibition of residence and a sentence of expulsion for a period of less than six months. |
|---|
|  | (4) In proceedings on an agreement on the admission of guilt and acceptance of punishment, the court may impose a sentence of imprisonment reduced by one third below the lower limit of the statutory sentencing range and, in the case of criminal offences referred to in paragraph 3(a), a sentence of imprisonment of not less than twenty years. |
|---|
|

| § 40 |

|---|
|  | Waiver of punishment |
|---|
|  | (1) Punishment of an offender for a misdemeanour, if it did not cause death or serious bodily harm, may be waived if |
|---|
|  | a) the offender has admitted the commission of the misdemeanour, regrets its commission and shows effective efforts towards reform and if, having regard to the nature of the misdemeanour committed and the previous life of the offender, it can reasonably be expected that the mere hearing of the case before the court will be sufficient for his reform, |
|---|
|  | b) the court accepts a guarantee for the reform of the offender and considers that, having regard to the educational influence of the person who offered the guarantee, the nature of the misdemeanour committed and the person of the offender, the imposition of punishment is not necessary, or |
|---|
|  | c) he committed the misdemeanour in a state of diminished responsibility and the court considers that the protective treatment which it simultaneously imposes will ensure the protection of society and the reform of the offender more effectively than punishment; this does not apply if he caused the state of diminished responsibility by the influence of an addictive substance. |
|---|
|  | (2) Punishment of an offender for a criminal offence may be waived if the criminal offence was committed by a person under duress in direct connection with the fact that a criminal offence of trafficking in human beings under § 179, a criminal offence of sexual abuse under §§ 201 to 202, a criminal offence of abuse of a close person and a person entrusted under § 208 or a criminal offence of production of child pornography under § 368 was committed against him. |
|---|
|  | (3) If punishment of the offender has been waived pursuant to paragraph 1 or 2, the offender shall be regarded as if he had not been convicted. |
|---|
|

| § 40a |

|---|
|  | Conditional Waiver of Punishment |
|---|
|  | (1) The court may conditionally waive punishment of an offender of a misdemeanor under § 171 para. 1 or 2, if it simultaneously imposes protective treatment on him under the conditions pursuant to § 73. |
|---|
|  | (2) The court shall determine a probationary period of up to two years for the offender; the probationary period begins to run on the day following the day on which the decision on the conditional waiver of punishment becomes final. |
|---|
|  | (3) If the offender, for whom punishment was conditionally waived, complied with the conditions of protective treatment, the court shall declare that he has proven himself; otherwise it shall decide, possibly already during the probationary period, on the imposition of punishment. |
|---|
|  | (4) If the court has not made a decision pursuant to paragraph 3 within one year from the expiry of the probationary period without the offender being at fault for this, he shall be deemed to have proven himself. |
|---|
|  | (5) If the court has declared that the offender, for whom punishment was conditionally waived, has proven himself, or if he is deemed to have proven himself, he shall be regarded as if he had not been convicted. |
|---|
|

| § 41 |

|---|
|  | Aggregate Sentence and Joint Sentence |
|---|
|  | (1) If the court convicts an offender of two or more criminal offenses, it shall impose an aggregate sentence according to the statutory provision that applies to the most severely punishable criminal offense among them. In addition to the punishment permissible under such statutory provision, another type of punishment may also be imposed within the aggregate sentence if its imposition would be justified by one of the concurrent criminal offenses. If the lower limits of the sentencing ranges for imprisonment are different, the lower limit of the aggregate sentence shall be the highest of them. |
|---|
|  | (2) If the court imposes an aggregate sentence of imprisonment for two or more intentional criminal offenses, of which at least one is a felony, committed by two or more acts, the upper limit of the sentencing range for imprisonment of the most severely punishable criminal offense among them shall be increased by one third. The upper limit of the increased sentencing range may not exceed twenty-five years and, in the case of juveniles, the sentencing range specified in § 117 para. 1 or 3. In addition to imprisonment, another type of punishment may also be imposed within the aggregate sentence if its imposition would be justified by one of the criminal offenses being tried. |
|---|
|  | (3) If the court convicts an offender of another partial attack that forms part of a continuing criminal offense, for another partial attack of which a conviction judgment was pronounced by the court of first instance that has already become final, it shall annul in the judgment the earlier finding of guilt regarding the continuing criminal offense and the criminal offenses committed in ideal concurrence with it, the entire pronouncement on punishment, as well as other pronouncements that have their basis in the said finding of guilt. The court, being bound by the factual findings in the annulled judgment, shall decide again on the guilt for the continuing criminal offense including the new partial attack, possibly for criminal offenses committed in ideal concurrence with it, as well as on the joint sentence for the continuing criminal offense, which may not be more lenient than the punishment imposed by the earlier judgment. The court shall also decide, where appropriate, on consequential pronouncements that have their basis in the finding of guilt. If a punishment is imposed for more criminal offenses, the provisions of paragraphs 1 and 2, § 42 and § 43 shall apply mutatis mutandis. |
|---|
|  | (4) The provision of paragraph 3 shall not apply if the previous judgment was issued by a court of another Member State of the European Union. |
|---|
|

| § 42 |

|---|
|  | Cumulative Sentence |
|---|
|  | (1) If the court convicts an offender of a criminal offense that he committed before a conviction judgment for another of his criminal offenses was pronounced by the court of first instance, it shall impose a cumulative sentence on him according to the principles for imposing an aggregate sentence. |
|---|
|  | (2) Together with the imposition of a cumulative sentence, the court shall annul the pronouncement on the punishment imposed on the offender by the earlier judgment, as well as all other decisions substantively consequential to this pronouncement, if, in view of the change that occurred by the annulment, they have lost their basis. The cumulative sentence may not be more lenient than the punishment imposed by the earlier judgment. Within the cumulative sentence, the court shall impose the punishment of loss of honorary titles and decorations, the punishment of loss of military and other rank, the punishment of forfeiture of property, a fine, the punishment of forfeiture of a thing, the punishment of prohibition of activity or the punishment of prohibition of participation in public events, if such punishment was already imposed by the earlier judgment and if this is not precluded by the provision of § 34 para. 7. |
|---|
|  | (3) The provision on cumulative sentence shall not apply if the earlier conviction is of such a nature that the offender is regarded as if he had not been convicted or if the earlier judgment was issued by a court of another Member State of the European Union. |
|---|
|

| § 43 |

|---|
|  | Additional Sentence |
|---|
|  | If the court convicts an offender of a criminal offense that he committed before the punishment imposed by the earlier judgment was executed, and imposes on him a punishment of the same type, this punishment together with the not yet executed part of the punishment imposed by the earlier judgment may not exceed the maximum measure permitted by this Act for this type of punishment. If one of these punishments is imprisonment, such maximum measure shall be understood as a period of twenty-five years or life imprisonment. |
|---|
|

| § 44 |

|---|
|  | Waiver of Cumulative Sentence and Additional Sentence |
|---|
|  | The court shall waive the imposition of a cumulative sentence pursuant to § 42 or the imposition of an additional sentence pursuant to § 43, if it considers the punishment imposed by the earlier judgment to be sufficient for the protection of society and the correction of the offender. |
|---|
|

| § 45 |

|---|
|  | Crediting of Detention and Punishment |
|---|
|  | (1) If criminal prosecution was conducted against the offender in detention and his conviction occurs in these proceedings, the period spent in detention shall be credited to him towards the imposed punishment, possibly towards the aggregate sentence or cumulative sentence, if crediting is possible in view of the type of punishment imposed. The same procedure shall be followed if the court waives the imposition of a cumulative sentence or additional sentence. |
|---|
|  | (2) If the offender was apprehended within the framework of criminal prosecution and his conviction occurs in these proceedings, the period of restriction of personal liberty shall be credited to him towards the imposed punishment, if crediting is possible in view of the type of punishment imposed. |
|---|
|  | (3) If the offender was punished by a court or other authority and his conviction occurred for the same act, the executed punishment shall be credited to him towards the imposed punishment, if crediting is possible in view of the type of punishment imposed. The court shall proceed in the same manner if it imposed an aggregate sentence or cumulative sentence on the offender. |
|---|
|  | (4) If crediting of detention or punishment pursuant to paragraphs 1 to 3 is not possible, the court shall take this fact into account when determining the type of punishment, possibly the measure of punishment. |
|---|
|  | (5) The court shall proceed according to the same principles also in the case where the offender was in detention abroad or if he was punished by an authority of a foreign state for the same act. |
|---|
|  | Part Three |
|---|
|  | Imposition and Execution of Individual Punishments |
|---|
|

| § 46 |

|---|
|  | Imprisonment |
|---|
|  | Imprisonment may be imposed as a punishment for a definite period, for a maximum of twenty-five years, or as life imprisonment. |
|---|
|

| § 47 |

|---|
|  | Life Imprisonment |
|---|
|  | (1) The court may impose a sentence of life imprisonment only for a criminal offence for which this law permits it in the special part, and only under the conditions that |
|---|
|  | a) the imposition of such a sentence is required for the effective protection of society and |
|---|
|  | b) there is no hope that the offender could be reformed by a sentence of imprisonment for a term of up to twenty-five years. |
|---|
|  | (2) If the court sentences an offender for a completed criminal offence of premeditated murder under § 144, murder under § 145, bodily harm under § 155, illicit production of narcotic and psychotropic substances, poisons or precursors, their possession and trafficking in them under § 172 para. 5, 6, 7 or 8, trafficking in human beings under § 179, entrusting a child to the power of another under § 180 para. 2 or 3 or under § 181, hostage-taking under § 185, abduction abroad under § 187, robbery under § 188, extortion under § 189 para. 2, 3 or 4, gross coercion under § 190 or § 191 para. 2, 3 or 4, rape under § 199, sexual violence under § 200, sexual abuse under § 201 para. 2, 3 or 4, abuse of a close person and entrusted person under § 208, general endangerment under § 284, endangering the safety of an aircraft and vessel under § 291, abduction of an aircraft abroad under § 293, establishment, conspiracy and support of a criminal group under § 296, establishment, conspiracy and support of a terrorist group under § 297, terror under § 313 or § 314, forcible crossing of a state border under § 354 para. 2, 3 or 4, smuggling of migrants under § 355 para. 3, 4 or 5, production of child pornography under § 368, genocide under § 418, terrorist attack under § 419, certain forms of participation in terrorism under § 419b, financing of terrorism under § 419c, travel for the purpose of terrorism under § 419d or inhumanity under § 425, who has already been punished twice for such criminal offences, even in the stage of attempt, by an unconditional sentence of imprisonment, it shall impose a sentence of life imprisonment if the conditions set out in paragraph 1 (a) and (b) are met; otherwise it shall impose a sentence of imprisonment for twenty-five years, unless circumstances worthy of special consideration prevent it. However, the court may not impose on such an offender a sentence of imprisonment below twenty years. |
|---|
|

| § 48 |

|---|
|  | External Differentiation of the Execution of Imprisonment |
|---|
|  | (1) Imprisonment shall be executed in a differentiated manner in institutions for the execution of imprisonment (hereinafter referred to as "institution for the execution of sentence") of minimum, medium or maximum security level. |
|---|
|  | (2) The court shall assign the offender to serve the sentence of imprisonment as a rule to an institution for the execution of sentence |
|---|
|  | a) with a minimum security level, if in the last ten years before the commission of the criminal offence he was not serving a sentence of imprisonment imposed on him for an intentional criminal offence, |
|---|
|  | b) with a medium security level, if in the last ten years before the commission of the criminal offence he was serving a sentence of imprisonment imposed on him for an intentional criminal offence; however, the previous conviction shall not be taken into account if the offender is regarded as not having been convicted. |
|---|
|  | (3) The court shall assign to an institution for the execution of sentence with a maximum security level |
|---|
|  | a) an offender on whom it has imposed a sentence of life imprisonment, or |
|---|
|  | b) an offender of a particularly serious felony. |
|---|
|  | (4) The court may also assign an offender to an institution for the execution of sentence of a different security level than that to which he is to be assigned under paragraph 2, if, having regard to the seriousness of the criminal offence and the degree of the offender's disturbance, it considers that his reform will be better ensured in an institution for the execution of sentence of a different security level. In this way, it may assign an offender to an institution for the execution of sentence of minimum security level even if he was serving a sentence for an intentional misdemeanour in the last ten years before the commission of the criminal offence. However, it may not assign to an institution for the execution of sentence with a minimum or medium security level an offender on whom a sentence of life imprisonment has been imposed, or an offender of a particularly serious felony on whom a sentence of imprisonment exceeding fifteen years has been imposed. |
|---|
|  | (5) When assigning an offender to an institution for the execution of sentence of minimum, medium or maximum security level, the court is not bound by the provisions of paragraphs 2 and 3 even if it approves an agreement on the admission of guilt and acceptance of punishment or reduces the sentence below the lower limit of the sentencing range under § 39 para. 2 (b). |
|---|
|  | Conditional Suspension of the Execution of Imprisonment |
|---|
|

| § 49 |

|---|
|  | (1) The court may conditionally suspend the execution of a sentence of imprisonment not exceeding two years if |
|---|
|  | a) having regard to the person of the offender, in particular taking into account his previous life and the environment in which he lives and works, and the circumstances of the case, it has reasonable grounds to believe that the execution of the sentence of imprisonment is not necessary to ensure the protection of society and the reform of the offender, or |
|---|
|  | b) it accepts a guarantee for the reform of the offender and if, having regard to the educational influence of the person who offered the guarantee, it considers that the execution of the sentence of imprisonment is not necessary. |
|---|
|  | (2) The provision of paragraph 1 shall not apply if the court sentences the offender for an intentional criminal offence committed during the probationary period of conditional sentencing or during the probationary period of conditional release from the execution of imprisonment. |
|---|
|

| § 50 |

|---|
|  | (1) When granting a conditional suspension of the execution of a custodial sentence, the court shall determine a probationary period of one to five years. The probationary period begins to run on the day following the day on which the judgment becomes final. The probationary period does not run during the execution of an unconditional custodial sentence and during the execution of custody. |
|---|
|  | (2) In granting a conditional suspension of the execution of a custodial sentence, the court may impose on the offender an obligation to compensate, according to his abilities, for the damage caused by the criminal offence or to pay a debt or overdue maintenance during the probationary period. |
|---|
|  | (3) The probationary period that has already elapsed shall be credited towards the probationary period newly determined when granting a conditional suspension of the execution of a custodial sentence imposed for the same act, or towards the probationary period determined when imposing an aggregate sentence or a joint sentence. |
|---|
|  | (4) If the convicted person has led a proper life during the probationary period and has fulfilled the obligation to compensate for the damage caused by the criminal offence or to pay a debt or overdue maintenance, if imposed, the court shall declare that he has proved himself; otherwise it shall decide, possibly already during the probationary period, that the custodial sentence shall be executed. The court shall proceed in the same manner also in the case of other restrictions or obligations under § 51(3) and (4), if imposed. If the convicted person is serving a custodial sentence, the court shall decide so that the executions of custodial sentences follow one another continuously. Exceptionally, the court may, having regard to the circumstances of the case, leave the conditional conviction in force even though the convicted person has, by conduct committed during the probationary period, given cause for ordering the execution of the sentence, and at the same time may |
|---|
|  | a) establish probation supervision over the convicted person and impose hitherto unimposed reasonable restrictions or reasonable obligations referred to in § 51(3) and (4) aimed at ensuring that he leads a proper life, |
|---|
|  | b) reasonably extend the probationary period, but not by more than two years, and must not exceed the upper limit of the probationary period established in paragraph 1. |
|---|
|  | (5) If the court does not make a decision under paragraph 4 within one year from the expiry of the probationary period without the convicted person being at fault, the convicted person shall be deemed to have proved himself. |
|---|
|  | (6) Likewise, the convicted person shall be deemed to have proved himself if the court does not make a decision under paragraph 4 within two years from the expiry of the probationary period without the convicted person being at fault in the event that criminal proceedings are being conducted against the convicted person during this period for another criminal offence committed during the probationary period. |
|---|
|  | (7) If it has been declared that the convicted person has proved himself during the probationary period, or if he is deemed to have proved himself, he shall be regarded as if he had not been convicted. |
|---|
|  | (8) If the court decides that the custodial sentence which was conditionally suspended shall be executed, it shall also decide on the manner of execution of the sentence. |
|---|
|  | Conditional suspension of the execution of a custodial sentence with probation supervision |
|---|
|

| § 51 |

|---|
|  | (1) The court may, under the conditions set out in § 49(1), conditionally suspend the execution of a custodial sentence not exceeding three years if it simultaneously imposes probation supervision over the offender's conduct during the probationary period. The provision of § 49(2) shall apply equally. |
|---|
|  | (2) When determining probation supervision, the court shall establish a probationary period of one to five years. The probationary period begins to run on the day following the day on which the judgment becomes final. At the same time, the court shall impose restrictions or obligations which form part of the probation supervision. The probationary period does not run during the execution of an unconditional custodial sentence and during the execution of custody. |
|---|
|  | (3) Restrictions consist in particular of a prohibition on |
|---|
|  | a) participation in specified public events, |
|---|
|  | b) consumption of alcoholic beverages and other addictive substances, |
|---|
|  | c) meeting with persons who have a negative influence on the offender or who were his co-perpetrators or participants in the criminal offence, |
|---|
|  | d) entry to designated places or premises where he committed the criminal offence, |
|---|
|  | e) participation in games of chance, playing on gaming machines and placing bets, |
|---|
|  | f) contact with a specified person in any form, including contact by means of an electronic communication service or other similar means. |
|---|
|  | (4) Obligations consist in particular of an order to |
|---|
|  | a) not approach a specified person to a distance of less than five metres and not stay in the vicinity of the dwelling of the specified person or in a specified place where such person stays or which he visits. |
|---|
|  | b) stay at a specified time at a specified address, |
|---|
|  | c) move out of an apartment or house in which he is staying unlawfully or which he has unlawfully occupied, |
|---|
|  | d) compensate for the damage caused during the probationary period, |
|---|
|  | e) pay a debt or overdue maintenance during the probationary period, |
|---|
|  | f) apologise to the injured party personally or publicly, |
|---|
|  | g) obtain specified work qualifications or participate in a retraining course during the probationary period, |
|---|
|  | h) undergo, in cooperation with a probation and mediation officer or other specialist, a social training programme or other educational programme, |
|---|
|  | i) undergo treatment for addiction to addictive substances if protective treatment has not been imposed on him, |
|---|
|  | j) undergo psychotherapy or participate in psychological counselling during the probationary period, |
|---|
|  | k) take up employment during the probationary period or demonstrably seek employment, |
|---|
|  | l) report at a specified time to the relevant unit of the Police Force determined according to the place of residence, in justified cases also repeatedly. |
|---|
|  | (5) An offender on whom probation supervision has been imposed is obliged to tolerate supervision carried out by a probation and mediation officer and to submit to supervision by technical means if such supervision is ordered; supervision by technical means may be ordered if the conditions under a special regulation are met. |
|---|
|  | (6) The probationary period under paragraph 2 does not apply to the execution of other sentences imposed in addition to probation supervision. |
|---|
|  | (7) The probationary period that has already elapsed shall be credited towards the probationary period that was determined when imposing new probation supervision imposed for the same act, or towards the probationary period determined when imposing an aggregate sentence or a joint sentence. |
|---|
|  | (8) When imposing a restriction under paragraph 3(f) and an obligation under paragraph 4(a), the court shall inform the specified person of the possibility of applying for the issue of a European protection order for the purpose of ensuring his protection in the territory of another Member State of the European Union under a special regulation. |
|---|
|  | (9) The obligation under paragraph 4(h) may be imposed after prior request for a report from the probation and mediation officer on the appropriateness of its imposition, if the appropriateness of imposing this obligation does not follow from an expert opinion or an expert report. |
|---|
|

| § 52 |

|---|
|  | (1) If the convicted person has led a proper life during the probationary period, complied with the conditions of probation supervision and fulfilled the imposed restrictions and obligations, the court shall declare that he or she has proven himself or herself; otherwise it shall order an unconditional sentence of imprisonment, possibly already during the probationary period. Exceptionally, the court may, having regard to the circumstances of the case, leave the probation supervision in force even though the convicted person by his or her conduct committed during the probationary period has given cause for ordering the execution of the sentence, and at the same time may |
|---|
|  | a) appropriately extend the probation supervision, but not by more than two years, provided that it does not exceed the upper limit of probation supervision established in § 51(2), |
|---|
|  | b) impose hitherto unimposed appropriate restrictions or obligations, or |
|---|
|  | c) order the monitoring of imposed appropriate restrictions or obligations by technical means, if the conditions under a special regulation are met and if such monitoring has not been ordered so far. |
|---|
|  | (2) If the court does not make a decision under paragraph 1 within one year from the expiry of the probationary period without the convicted person being at fault, it shall be deemed that the convicted person has proven himself or herself. |
|---|
|  | (3) Likewise, it shall be deemed that the convicted person has proven himself or herself if the court does not make a decision under paragraph 1 within two years from the expiry of the probationary period without the convicted person being at fault in the event that criminal proceedings are being conducted against the convicted person during this period for another criminal offence committed during the probationary period. |
|---|
|  | (4) If it has been declared that the convicted person has proven himself or herself during the probationary period, or if it is deemed that he or she has proven himself or herself, he or she shall be regarded as if he or she had not been convicted. |
|---|
|  | (5) If the court converts a conditional sentence of imprisonment into an unconditional sentence of imprisonment, it shall at the same time decide on the manner of execution of the sentence. |
|---|
|

| § 53 |

|---|
|  | Sentence of house arrest |
|---|
|  | (1) The court may impose a sentence of house arrest of up to four years on the offender of a criminal offence if |
|---|
|  | a) having regard to the nature of the criminal offence referred to in paragraph 2, the person and circumstances of the offender, the imposition of this sentence is sufficient, |
|---|
|  | b) the offender has given a written promise that he or she will stay at a residence at a specified address at a specified time and will provide the necessary cooperation during the execution of monitoring, |
|---|
|  | c) the conditions for the execution of monitoring by technical means are met. |
|---|
|  | (2) The court may impose a sentence of house arrest in the amount according to paragraph 1 for a criminal offence with an upper limit of the sentence rate established by this Act not exceeding ten years, but at least at the lower limit of the sentence rate of imprisonment established by this Act. |
|---|
|  | (3) The convicted person during the execution of the sentence of house arrest is obliged, at the time determined by the court, to stay in his or her residence including the outdoor areas belonging to it, to lead a proper life and to submit to monitoring by technical means. |
|---|
|  | (4) The court may impose on the offender during the execution of the sentence of house arrest restrictions or obligations referred to in § 51(3) and (4) aimed at ensuring that he or she leads a proper life. |
|---|
|  | (5) During the execution of the sentence of house arrest, the convicted person may leave his or her residence only with the prior consent of the probation and mediation officer, and only for a compelling reason and for the time strictly necessary. This time shall be counted towards the execution of the sentence. |
|---|
|  | (6) If the convicted person fails to comply with the restrictions or obligations arising from the sentence of house arrest or frustrates the execution of monitoring by technical means, the court shall convert the sentence into an unconditional sentence of imprisonment so that one day of the unexecuted sentence of house arrest equals one day of unconditional imprisonment, and at the same time shall decide on the manner of execution of this sentence. |
|---|
|  | Sentence of community service |
|---|
|

| § 54 |

|---|
|  | The court may impose a sentence of community service with the consent of the offender in the amount of 40 to 300 hours if it convicts him or her of a misdemeanour for which the law allows the imposition of a sentence of imprisonment whose upper limit of the sentence rate of imprisonment does not exceed five years. |
|---|
|

| § 55 |

|---|
|  | (1) The convicted person is obliged to execute the sentence of community service no later than one year from the order to execute this sentence. The court may also impose on the offender for this period appropriate restrictions or obligations referred to in § 51(3) and (4), aimed at ensuring that he or she leads a proper life, and shall as a rule impose on him or her the obligation to compensate, according to his or her strength and abilities, for the damage caused by the criminal offence. The period of execution of the sentence of community service shall not include the time during which the convicted person |
|---|
|  | a) could not execute the sentence of community service due to temporary incapacity for work or because work was not assigned to him or her during this period, |
|---|
|  | b) performed compulsory military service or other service instead of compulsory military service, |
|---|
|  | c) stayed abroad, |
|---|
|  | d) was in custody or serving a sentence of imprisonment in another case. |
|---|
|  | (2) The court shall not impose a sentence of community service if the offender is long-term incapacitated for work or disabled. |
|---|
|  | (3) The convicted person is obliged to execute the sentence of community service personally and in his or her free time without entitlement to remuneration. |
|---|
|  | (4) If the convicted person during the execution of the sentence of community service did not lead a proper life or culpably failed to perform the work to the specified extent, or failed to comply with the imposed restrictions or obligations, the court shall convert the sentence of community service or its remainder into a sentence of imprisonment so that for every two hours of unperformed work it shall order one day of unconditional imprisonment and at the same time shall decide on the manner of execution of this sentence. |
|---|
|  | (5) The court may waive the execution of the sentence of community service if the convicted person during the execution of this sentence, through no fault of his or her own, became permanently incapacitated for work or disabled or for other serious reasons. |
|---|
|  | Pecuniary sentence |
|---|
|

| § 56 |

|---|
|  | (1) The court may impose a pecuniary sentence from 160 euros to 33 1930 euros on the offender of an intentional criminal offence by which he or she obtained or attempted to obtain a pecuniary advantage. |
|---|
|  | (2) Without fulfilling the conditions referred to in paragraph 1, the court may impose a pecuniary sentence if it imposes it for a misdemeanour and, having regard to the nature of the misdemeanour committed and the possibility of reforming the offender, it does not impose a sentence of imprisonment. |
|---|
|  | (3) If justified by the amount of the imposed pecuniary sentence and the personal and property circumstances of the offender, the court may decide that the convicted person shall pay the pecuniary sentence in monthly instalments. At the same time, it shall determine their amount and a period of no more than one year from the entry into force of the convicting judgment within which the pecuniary sentence must be paid. |
|---|
|  | (4) A pecuniary sentence which the convicted person has already served shall be credited towards a new pecuniary sentence which was imposed on the offender for the same act, or towards this sentence if it was imposed as a cumulative sentence or aggregate sentence. |
|---|
|  | (5) The court shall not impose a pecuniary sentence if this would frustrate the possibility of compensation for damage caused by the criminal offence. |
|---|
|

| § 57 |

|---|
|  | (1) When imposing a pecuniary sentence, the court shall take into account the personal and property circumstances of the offender. It shall not impose a pecuniary sentence if it is clear that the convicted person will not be able to pay it. |
|---|
|  | (2) The paid amount of the pecuniary sentence shall accrue to the State. |
|---|
|  | (3) If the court imposes a pecuniary sentence, it shall establish, for the case that the execution of the pecuniary sentence could be intentionally frustrated, a substitute sentence of imprisonment of up to five years. The substitute sentence must not, together with the imposed sentence of imprisonment, exceed the legally permitted limit of the sentence rate. |
|---|
|  | (4) If the substitute sentence would exceed the limit referred to in paragraph 3 or if the pecuniary sentence is imposed in addition to a sentence of life imprisonment, the court shall not impose a substitute sentence. |
|---|
|  | Sentence of forfeiture of property |
|---|
|

| § 58 |

|---|
|  | (1) The court may impose the penalty of forfeiture of property having regard to the circumstances of the criminal offence committed and the situation of the offender, if it sentences the offender to life imprisonment or if it sentences him to an unconditional term of imprisonment for a particularly serious felony by which the offender obtained or attempted to obtain pecuniary advantage on a large scale or by which he caused damage on a large scale. |
|---|
|  | (2) The court shall impose the penalty of forfeiture of property without fulfilling the conditions set out in paragraph 1, if it sentences the offender for committing the criminal offence of illicit production of narcotic and psychotropic substances, poisons or precursors, their possession and trafficking in them under § 173(3), the criminal offence of trafficking in human beings under § 179, the criminal offence of entrusting a child to the power of another under § 180(2) or (3) or § 181, the criminal offence of extortion under § 189(2)(c), the criminal offence of gross coercion under § 190(1), (3), (4) or (5) or § 191(3) or (4), the criminal offence of coercion under § 192(3) or (4), the criminal offence of unauthorised operation of lotteries and other similar games under § 230(2), (3) or (4), the criminal offence of legalisation of proceeds of crime under § 234, the criminal offence of counterfeiting, alteration and unauthorised production of money and securities under § 270, the criminal offence of putting into circulation counterfeit, altered and unauthorised produced money and securities under § 271(1), the criminal offence of production and possession of counterfeiting instruments under § 272(3), the criminal offence of evasion of tax and insurance contributions under § 276(3) or (4), the criminal offence of non-payment of tax and insurance contributions under § 277(3) or (4), the criminal offence of tax fraud under § 277a(2) or (3), the criminal offence of failure to pay tax and insurance contributions under § 278(2) or (3), the criminal offence of breach of regulations on state technical measures for marking goods under § 279(2) or (3), the criminal offence of terror under § 313 or § 314, the criminal offence of accepting a bribe under § 328(2), the criminal offence of counterfeiting and alteration of a public document, official seal, official closure, official emblem and official mark under § 352(6), the criminal offence of smuggling of migrants under § 355 or § 356, the criminal offence of procuring under § 367(3), the criminal offence of production of child pornography under § 368, the criminal offence of distribution of child pornography under § 369, the criminal offence of endangering morality under § 372(2) or (3), the criminal offence of terrorist attack under § 419, the criminal offence of certain forms of participation in terrorism under § 419b, the criminal offence of financing of terrorism under § 419c or the criminal offence of travel for the purpose of terrorism under § 419d and the offender acquired property at least on a substantial scale by criminal activity or from proceeds of crime. |
|---|
