|  | (3) The court shall impose the penalty of forfeiture of property without fulfilling the conditions set out in paragraph 1 also if it sentences the offender for committing the criminal offence of illicit production of narcotic and psychotropic substances, poisons or precursors, their possession and trafficking in them under § 172(7) or (8) or § 173(4), the criminal offence of legalisation of proceeds of crime under § 233(3) or (4), the criminal offence of founding, conspiring and supporting a criminal group under § 296, the criminal offence of founding, conspiring and supporting a terrorist group under § 297, the criminal offence of accepting a bribe under § 328(3) or § 329(3) or the criminal offence of bribery under § 334(2). |
|---|
|

| § 59 |

|---|
|  | (1) The penalty of forfeiture of property affects, to the extent belonging to the convicted person upon execution of the penalty of forfeiture of property after completion of bankruptcy proceedings, |
|---|
|  | a) the proceeds from the realisation of property, |
|---|
|  | b) property excluded from the inventory of the bankruptcy estate, |
|---|
|  | c) property subject to bankruptcy, if the property has not been realised. |
|---|
|  | (2) The State becomes the owner of the forfeited property, unless the court decides otherwise on the basis of a promulgated international treaty by which the Slovak Republic is bound. |
|---|
|  | (3) By a final decision of the court on forfeiture of property, the community property of spouses ceases to exist. Property in the debtor's community property of spouses belongs to the bankruptcy estate if it has not yet been settled. The settlement of community property of spouses that occurred earlier, including acts that changed its scope, may be contested in bankruptcy. |
|---|
|

| § 60 |

|---|
|  | Penalty of forfeiture of a thing |
|---|
|  | (1) The court shall impose the penalty of forfeiture of a thing, |
|---|
|  | a) which was used to commit a criminal offence, |
|---|
|  | b) which was intended for the commission of a criminal offence, or |
|---|
|  | c) which is proceeds of crime. |
|---|
|  | (2) If the thing referred to in paragraph 1 is unattainable or unidentifiable, or is mixed with the offender's property or with property of another person acquired in accordance with the law, the court may impose forfeiture of a thing of such value as corresponds to the value of that thing. |
|---|
|  | (3) An unattainable thing means a thing destroyed, damaged, lost, stolen, rendered unusable, consumed, concealed, transferred to another person with the aim of removing it from the scope of authorities active in criminal proceedings or otherwise removed or costs saved. |
|---|
|  | (4) The court may impose the penalty of forfeiture of a thing only if it is a thing belonging to the offender. |
|---|
|  | (5) The State becomes the owner of the forfeited thing, unless the court decides otherwise on the basis of a promulgated international treaty by which the Slovak Republic is bound. |
|---|
|  | (6) The provision of paragraph 1 shall not apply if |
|---|
|  | a) the injured party has acquired from the act a claim for compensation for damage, the satisfaction of which would be prevented by forfeiture of the thing, |
|---|
|  | b) the value of the thing is in manifest disproportion to the degree of seriousness of the misdemeanour, or |
|---|
|  | c) the court has waived punishment of the offender. |
|---|
|

| § 61 |

|---|
|  | Prohibition of Activity Penalty |
|---|
|  | (1) The prohibition of activity penalty consists in prohibiting the convicted person, for the duration of the execution of this penalty, from exercising a particular employment, profession or function, or such activity for which special permission is required or whose conditions of exercise are regulated by a special regulation. |
|---|
|  | (2) The court may impose the prohibition of activity penalty for one year to ten years if the offender committed a criminal offence in connection with this activity. |
|---|
|  | (3) If the court convicts an offender of the criminal offence of endangerment under the influence of an addictive substance pursuant to § 289, which he committed as a driver of a means of transport, who has already been convicted of such a criminal offence or of the criminal offence of causing death pursuant to § 149 para. 4 or 5, which he committed as a driver of a means of transport, it shall impose the prohibition of activity penalty in the upper half of the penalty rate specified in paragraph 2. |
|---|
|  | (4) The court may impose the prohibition of activity penalty for life if it convicts an offender of the criminal offence of trafficking in human beings pursuant to § 179, the criminal offence of rape pursuant to § 199, the criminal offence of sexual violence pursuant to § 200, the criminal offence of sexual abuse pursuant to § 201 to 202, the criminal offence of production of child pornography pursuant to § 368, the criminal offence of distribution of child pornography pursuant to § 369 and the criminal offence of possession of child pornography and participation in a child pornographic performance pursuant to § 370, if such criminal offence was committed against a child. |
|---|
|  | (5) The court shall impose the prohibition of activity penalty for life if it convicts an offender of the criminal offence of |
|---|
|  | a) endangerment under the influence of an addictive substance pursuant to § 289, which he committed as a driver of a means of transport, although he has already been convicted twice of such a criminal offence which he committed as a driver of a means of transport, or |
|---|
|  | b) causing death pursuant to § 149 para. 4 or 5, which he committed as a driver of a means of transport. |
|---|
|  | (6) If the court convicts an offender of the criminal offence of tax and insurance evasion pursuant to § 276 para. 2, 3 or para. 4, the criminal offence of non-remittance of tax and insurance pursuant to § 277 para. 2, 3 or para. 4, the criminal offence of tax fraud pursuant to § 277a para. 2 or para. 3, the criminal offence of non-payment of tax and insurance pursuant to § 278 para. 2 or para. 3 and the criminal offence of frustrating the execution of tax administration pursuant to § 278a para. 2 or para. 3, it shall impose the prohibition of activity penalty in the upper half of the penalty rate specified in paragraph 2. |
|---|
|  | (7) The expungement of conviction has no effect on the imposition of the prohibition of activity penalty pursuant to paragraphs 3 to 5. |
|---|
|  | (8) The period of execution of an unconditional custodial sentence and the period of execution of detention shall not be counted towards the period of execution of the prohibition of activity penalty; however, the period during which the offender was deprived of authorisation for the activity which is the subject of the prohibition before the judgment became final pursuant to special regulations, and the period during which he was no longer allowed to carry out this activity on the basis of a measure by a state authority, shall be counted. |
|---|
|  | (9) The prohibition of activity penalty already served by the convicted person shall be counted towards the new prohibition of activity penalty imposed on the offender for the same act or towards this penalty if it was imposed as a cumulative penalty or aggregate penalty. |
|---|
|  | (10) A final judgment imposing a prohibition of activity penalty to exercise the function of a member of a statutory body, a member of a supervisory body, head of an organisational unit of an undertaking, head of an undertaking of a foreign person, head of an organisational unit of an undertaking of a foreign person or procurator shall be a decision on exclusion pursuant to commercial law regulations for the duration of the execution of the penalty. |
|---|
|

| § 62 |

|---|
|  | Prohibition of Residence Penalty |
|---|
|  | (1) The prohibition of residence penalty consists in the convicted person not being allowed, for the duration of the execution of this penalty, to stay in a designated place or in a designated area; permission is required for temporary residence in such place or in such area for an essential personal matter. |
|---|
|  | (2) The court may impose the prohibition of residence penalty for one year to five years for an intentional criminal offence if the protection of public order, family, health, morality or property so requires with regard to the offender's previous way of life and the place where the act was committed. The prohibition of residence penalty may not apply to the place or area where the offender has permanent residence. |
|---|
|  | (3) The court may impose on the offender, for the duration of the execution of this penalty, reasonable restrictions and obligations specified in § 51 para. 3 and 4, aimed at ensuring that he leads a proper life, if it does not impose this penalty in addition to an unconditional custodial sentence. |
|---|
|  | (4) The convicted person is obliged to submit to control by technical means if such control is ordered. Control by technical means may be ordered if the conditions pursuant to a special regulation are met. |
|---|
|  | (5) The period of execution of a custodial sentence shall not be counted towards the period of execution of the prohibition of residence penalty. |
|---|
|  | (6) The prohibition of residence penalty may not be imposed in addition to the community service penalty if the work is to be performed in a place where the convicted person is prohibited from residing. |
|---|
|

| § 62a |

|---|
|  | Prohibition of Attendance at Public Events Penalty |
|---|
|  | (1) The prohibition of attendance at public events penalty consists in prohibiting the convicted person, during the period of execution of this penalty, from attending sporting, cultural or other public events to the extent specified in the court's decision. |
|---|
|  | (2) The court may impose the prohibition of attendance at public events penalty for up to ten years if the offender commits an intentional criminal offence in connection with attendance at a public event or if the protection of public order, health, morality or property so requires with regard to the offender's previous way of life and the circumstances of the commission of the act. |
|---|
|  | (3) The prohibition of attendance at public events penalty may be imposed as an independent penalty if, having regard to the nature and seriousness of the misdemeanour committed, the person and circumstances of the offender, the imposition of another penalty is not necessary. |
|---|
|  | (4) For the period of execution of the prohibition of attendance at public events penalty or for a part thereof, the court may impose on the convicted person the restrictions and obligations specified in § 51 para. 3 and 4, in particular that he report at a specified time to the relevant unit of the Police Force, and as a rule the court shall also impose on him that he compensate, according to his means, for the damage caused by the criminal offence or a part thereof, which the court shall also determine. |
|---|
|  | (5) The convicted person is obliged to submit to control by technical means if such control is ordered. Control by technical means may be ordered if the conditions pursuant to a special regulation are met. |
|---|
|  | (6) The period of execution of an unconditional custodial sentence shall not be counted towards the period of execution of the prohibition of attendance at public events penalty; however, the period during which attendance at public events which are the subject of the prohibition was prohibited to the offender in the same case before the judgment became final shall be counted. |
|---|
|

| § 63 |

|---|
|  | Loss of Honorary Titles and Decorations Penalty |
|---|
|  | (1) The loss of honorary titles and decorations penalty consists in the convicted person losing decorations and other honorary titles awarded pursuant to special regulations. |
|---|
|  | (2) The court may impose the loss of honorary titles and decorations penalty if it convicts an offender of a particularly serious felony to a custodial sentence exceeding five years. |
|---|
|  | (3) The court may impose this penalty also in addition to a shorter custodial sentence or in addition to another penalty if, having regard to the nature of the particularly serious felony committed, the protection of the dignity of the honorary title and decoration which were awarded to the offender before conviction so requires. |
|---|
|

| § 64 |

|---|
|  | Penalty of Loss of Military and Other Rank |
|---|
|  | (1) The penalty of loss of military and other rank consists in reducing the convicted person's rank in the armed forces to the rank of soldier, and in the case of a member in service, his rank in that corps shall be taken away. |
|---|
|  | (2) The court shall impose the penalty of loss of military and other rank on an offender who holds such rank if it sentences him for an intentional criminal offence to a penalty of imprisonment of at least two years without conditional suspension of its execution. |
|---|
|  | (3) The court may impose this penalty also in addition to a shorter penalty of imprisonment or in addition to another penalty if it sentences an offender who is a member of the armed forces or a member in service, and if discipline and order in the armed forces or in service so require in view of the nature of the criminal offence committed. |
|---|
|

| § 65 |

|---|
|  | Penalty of Expulsion |
|---|
|  | (1) If required by the safety of persons or property or other public interest, the court may impose on an offender who is not a citizen of the Slovak Republic nor a person who has been granted asylum or provided with subsidiary protection, a penalty of expulsion from the territory of the Slovak Republic. |
|---|
|  | (2) The penalty of expulsion may not be imposed on an offender, |
|---|
|  | a) whose nationality or the state from which he travelled to the territory of the Slovak Republic could not be ascertained, |
|---|
|  | b) who is a citizen of a Member State of the European Union or a citizen of a Contracting State of the Agreement on the European Economic Area or his family member regardless of nationality and has permanent residence in the territory of the Slovak Republic; this does not apply if such person represents a threat to state security or public order, |
|---|
|  | c) who is a citizen of a Member State of the European Union or a citizen of a Contracting State of the Agreement on the European Economic Area with continuous residence in the territory of the Slovak Republic for more than ten years; this does not apply if such person represents a threat to state security, |
|---|
|  | d) who is a child and is a citizen of a Member State of the European Union or a citizen of a Contracting State of the Agreement on the European Economic Area, if expulsion is not in the best interests of the child, |
|---|
|  | e) if he is to be expelled to a state in which his personal freedom would be endangered on grounds of his race, nationality, religion, membership of a particular social group or political opinion; this does not apply to a person who can reasonably be considered dangerous to the security of the Slovak Republic, or to a person who has been convicted of a particularly serious crime and represents a danger to the Slovak Republic or |
|---|
|  | f) if he is to be expelled to a state in which his life would be endangered on grounds of his race, nationality, religion, membership of a particular social group or political opinion, or in which he would face torture, cruel, inhuman or degrading treatment or punishment, or in which the death penalty has been imposed on him or there is a presumption that in ongoing criminal proceedings such penalty may be imposed on him. |
|---|
|  | (3) A family member within the meaning of paragraph 2 letter b) is a family member within the meaning of regulations on the residence of foreigners. |
|---|
|  | (4) The court may impose the penalty of expulsion for one year to fifteen years. |
|---|
|  | Part Four |
|---|
|  | Conversion of the Remainder of a Penalty of Imprisonment into a Penalty of House Arrest, Conditional Release and Conditional Waiver of Execution of the Remainder of Certain Penalties |
|---|
|

| § 65a |

|---|
|  | Conversion of the Remainder of a Penalty of Imprisonment into a Penalty of House Arrest |
|---|
|  | (1) The court may convert the remainder of a penalty of imprisonment into a penalty of house arrest for a convicted person for a misdemeanour if the conditions under § 53 paragraph 1 are met and at the same time |
|---|
|  | a) the convicted person has demonstrated improvement in the execution of the penalty by fulfilling his obligations and by his conduct, |
|---|
|  | b) the convicted person has served one third of the imposed penalty of imprisonment or penalty of imprisonment reduced by decision of the President of the Slovak Republic, |
|---|
|  | c) the remainder of the unserved penalty of imprisonment does not exceed three years, |
|---|
|  | d) it is not the execution of a penalty that was ordered after a decision on failure during the probationary period determined in conditional suspension of execution of a penalty of imprisonment, conditional suspension of execution of a penalty of imprisonment with probation supervision, or the execution of the remainder of a penalty that was ordered after a decision on failure during the probationary period in conditional release from execution of a penalty of imprisonment, |
|---|
|  | e) the convicted person has not had a penalty of house arrest converted into a penalty of imprisonment and |
|---|
|  | f) the convicted person was not in execution of a penalty of imprisonment before committing the criminal offence. |
|---|
|  | (2) The court shall convert the remainder of the penalty of imprisonment into a penalty of house arrest so that one unserved day of the remainder of the penalty of imprisonment equals one day of the penalty of house arrest. |
|---|
|  | Conditional Release from Execution of a Penalty of Imprisonment |
|---|
|

| § 66 |

|---|
|  | (1) The court may conditionally release a convicted person if the convicted person has demonstrated improvement in the execution of the penalty by fulfilling his obligations and by his conduct and it can be expected that he will lead a proper life in the future, and |
|---|
|  | a) if it concerns a person convicted of a misdemeanour after serving half of the imposed unconditional penalty of imprisonment or unconditional penalty of imprisonment reduced by decision of the President of the Slovak Republic, |
|---|
|  | b) if it concerns a person convicted of a felony after serving two thirds of the imposed unconditional penalty of imprisonment or unconditional penalty of imprisonment reduced by decision of the President of the Slovak Republic, |
|---|
|  | c) if it concerns a person convicted of a felony who was not in execution of a penalty of imprisonment before committing the criminal offence after serving half of the imposed unconditional penalty of imprisonment or unconditional penalty of imprisonment reduced by decision of the President of the Slovak Republic; the court shall at the same time order monitoring by technical means. |
|---|
|  | (2) In deciding on conditional release, the court shall also take into account the nature of the criminal offence committed and the institution for execution of penalty in which the convicted person is serving the penalty. |
|---|
|  | (3) If the convicted person has to serve several penalties of imprisonment, the convicted person may be conditionally released at the earliest after serving the sum of the proportional parts of the imposed penalties according to paragraph 1 letters a) to c), § 67 paragraphs 1 and 2 and the entire remainder of the penalty according to § 68 paragraph 2. |
|---|
|  | (4) In the case of conditional release according to paragraph 1 letter c), monitoring by technical means may be terminated at the earliest after the expiry of a period corresponding to two thirds of the imposed unconditional penalty of imprisonment or unconditional penalty of imprisonment reduced by decision of the President of the Slovak Republic. |
|---|
|

| § 67 |

|---|
|  | (1) A person convicted of a particularly serious crime or a person sentenced to a penalty of imprisonment according to § 47 paragraph 2 except a person sentenced to life imprisonment may be conditionally released only after serving three quarters of the imposed penalty of imprisonment. |
|---|
|  | (2) A person sentenced to life imprisonment may be conditionally released at the earliest after twenty-five years of execution of this penalty, if, having regard to the circumstances of the act for which he was convicted and the nature of his personality, there is no risk that his release will result in repetition of the committed act or a similar act. |
|---|
|

| § 68 |

|---|
|  | (1) In the case of conditional release, the court shall determine a probationary period of one to seven years; the probationary period begins with the conditional release of the convicted person. The court may at the same time order probation supervision over the convicted person for a period of up to three years and impose appropriate restrictions or obligations referred to in § 51(3) and (4). If it concerns conditional release from the execution of a sentence of imprisonment for twenty-five years or for life, the court shall determine a probationary period of ten years and at the same time order probation supervision over the convicted person for a period of up to five years and impose appropriate restrictions or obligations referred to in § 51(3) and (4). The conditionally released person is obliged to submit to monitoring by technical means if such monitoring is ordered. Monitoring by technical means may be ordered if the conditions under a special regulation are met. The probationary period does not run during the execution of an unconditional sentence of imprisonment and during detention on remand. |
|---|
|  | (2) If the conditionally released person led a proper life during the probationary period and fulfilled the imposed restrictions and obligations, the court shall declare that he has proved himself; otherwise it shall decide, possibly already during the probationary period, that the remainder of the sentence shall be executed. If the convicted person is serving a sentence of imprisonment, the court shall decide so that the executions of sentences of imprisonment follow one another continuously. |
|---|
|  | (3) If the court has declared that the conditionally released person has proved himself, the sentence shall be deemed to have been executed on the day when he was conditionally released. |
|---|
|  | (4) Likewise, the sentence shall be deemed to have been executed on the day when the conditional release took place if the court, through no fault of the convicted person, has not decided within one year from the expiry of the probationary period that the convicted person shall execute the remainder of the sentence. The court may so decide within two years if criminal proceedings are being conducted against the accused for an intentional criminal offence committed during the probationary period of conditional release. |
|---|
|  | (5) Repeated conditional release from the execution of the same sentence is not possible. |
|---|
|  | Conditional waiver of execution of the remainder of a sentence of home detention |
|---|
|

| § 68a |

|---|
|  | (1) After execution of half of a sentence of home detention, the court may conditionally waive the execution of its remainder if the convicted person, by his way of life during the execution of the sentence, has demonstrated that further execution of this sentence is no longer necessary. |
|---|
|  | (2) In the case of conditional waiver of execution of the remainder of a sentence of home detention, the court shall determine a probationary period of up to two years, but not shorter than the remainder of the sentence; the probationary period begins to run on the day following the day on which the decision on conditional waiver of execution of the remainder of the sentence of home detention became final. |
|---|
|  | (3) The court may impose on the convicted person in respect of whom it has conditionally waived the execution of the remainder of the sentence of home detention appropriate restrictions or obligations referred to in § 51(3) and (4), aimed at ensuring that he leads a proper life. If it does not concern an obligation to compensate for damage caused by the criminal offence or to pay a debt or overdue maintenance, it shall order probation supervision. |
|---|
|

| § 68b |

|---|
|  | (1) If the convicted person in respect of whom the remainder of the sentence of home detention was conditionally waived led a proper life during the probationary period and fulfilled the imposed restrictions or obligations, the court shall declare that he has proved himself; otherwise it shall decide, possibly already during the probationary period, that the remainder of the sentence shall be converted into an unconditional sentence of imprisonment such that one day of the unexecuted sentence of home detention equals one day of unconditional sentence of imprisonment, and at the same time decide on the manner of execution of this sentence. |
|---|
|  | (2) If the court has declared that the convicted person has proved himself, the sentence shall be deemed to have been executed on the day when the decision on waiver of execution of the remainder of the sentence of home detention became final. |
|---|
|  | (3) Likewise, the sentence of home detention shall be deemed to have been executed on the day when the decision on conditional waiver of execution of the remainder of the sentence of home detention became final if the court, without the convicted person being at fault, has not decided on the conversion of the sentence under paragraph 1 within one year from the expiry of the probationary period, or if criminal proceedings are being conducted against the convicted person for another criminal offence committed during the probationary period, within two years from the expiry of the probationary period. |
|---|
|  | Conditional waiver of execution of the remainder of a sentence of prohibition of activity |
|---|
|

| § 69 |

|---|
|  | (1) After execution of half of a sentence of prohibition of activity, the court may conditionally waive the execution of its remainder if the convicted person, by his way of life during the execution of the sentence, has demonstrated that further execution of this sentence is no longer necessary. |
|---|
|  | (2) In the case of conditional waiver of execution of the remainder of a sentence of prohibition of activity, the court shall determine a probationary period of up to five years, but not shorter than the remainder of the sentence; the probationary period begins to run on the day following the day on which the decision on this waiver became final. |
|---|
|  | (3) The court may impose on the convicted person in respect of whom it has conditionally waived the execution of the remainder of the sentence of prohibition of activity appropriate restrictions and obligations referred to in § 51(3) and (4), aimed at ensuring that he leads a proper life; as a rule, it shall also impose on him an obligation to compensate, according to his abilities, for the damage caused by the criminal offence or a part thereof, which it shall determine at the same time. |
|---|
|  | (4) The court may not conditionally waive the execution of the remainder of a sentence of prohibition of activity under paragraph 1 if it concerns a sentence of prohibition of the activity of driving motor vehicles that was imposed on a convicted person, |
|---|
|  | a) on whom such a sentence of prohibition of activity has already been imposed in the preceding ten years for a criminal offence of endangerment under the influence of an addictive substance or for another criminal offence committed under the influence of an addictive substance, or |
|---|
|  | b) who has already been punished in the preceding ten years by a prohibition of the activity of driving motor vehicles for a similar act committed under the influence of an addictive substance as referred to in letter a). |
|---|
|  | (5) The court may not conditionally waive the execution of the remainder of a sentence of prohibition of activity under paragraph 1 if the sentence of prohibition of activity was imposed under § 61(3) to (5). |
|---|
|

| § 70 |

|---|
|  | (1) If the convicted person in respect of whom the remainder of the sentence of prohibition of activity was conditionally waived led a proper life during the probationary period and fulfilled the imposed restrictions and obligations, the court shall declare that he has proved himself; otherwise it shall decide, possibly already during the probationary period, that the remainder of the sentence shall be executed. |
|---|
|  | (2) If the court has declared that the convicted person has proved himself, the sentence shall be deemed to have been executed on the day when the decision on waiver of execution of the remainder of the sentence of prohibition of activity became final. |
|---|
|  | (3) Likewise, the sentence of prohibition of activity shall be deemed to have been executed on the day when the decision on conditional waiver of execution of the remainder of the sentence of prohibition of activity became final if the court, through no fault of the convicted person, has not ordered within one year from the expiry of the probationary period that the convicted person shall execute the remainder of the sentence. |
|---|
|  | Conditional waiver of execution of the remainder of a sentence of prohibition of residence |
|---|
|

| § 71 |

|---|
|  | (1) After execution of half of a sentence of prohibition of residence, the court may conditionally waive the execution of its remainder if the convicted person, by his way of life during the execution of the sentence, has demonstrated that further execution of this sentence is no longer necessary. |
|---|
|  | (2) In the case of conditional waiver of execution of the remainder of a sentence of prohibition of residence, the court shall determine a probationary period of up to five years, but not shorter than the remainder of the sentence; the probationary period begins to run on the day following the day on which the decision on this waiver became final. |
|---|
|  | (3) The court may impose on the convicted person in respect of whom it has conditionally waived the execution of the remainder of the sentence of prohibition of residence appropriate restrictions and obligations referred to in § 51(3) and (4), aimed at ensuring that he leads a proper life; as a rule, it shall also impose on him an obligation to compensate, according to his abilities, for the damage caused by the criminal offence or a part thereof, which it shall determine at the same time. |
|---|
|

| § 72 |

|---|
|  | (1) If a convicted person, from whom the remainder of the sentence of prohibition of residence has been conditionally remitted, has led a proper life during the probationary period and has fulfilled the imposed restrictions and obligations, the court shall declare that he or she has proved himself or herself; otherwise it shall decide, possibly already during the probationary period, that the remainder of the sentence shall be executed. |
|---|
|  | (2) If the court has declared that the conditionally released person has proved himself or herself, the sentence shall be deemed to have been executed on the day on which the decision on remission of the execution of the remainder of the sentence of prohibition of residence became final. |
|---|
|  | (3) Likewise, the sentence of prohibition of residence shall be deemed to have been executed on the day on which the decision on conditional remission of the execution of the remainder of the sentence of prohibition of residence became final, if the court, through no fault of the convicted person, has not ordered within one year from the expiry of the probationary period that the convicted person shall execute the remainder of the sentence. |
|---|
|

| § 72a |

|---|
|  | Conditional remission of the execution of the remainder of the sentence of prohibition of participation in public events |
|---|
|  | (1) After the execution of half of the sentence of prohibition of participation in public events, the court may conditionally remit the execution of its remainder if the convicted person has demonstrated by his or her way of life during the execution of the sentence that further execution of this sentence is no longer necessary. |
|---|
|  | (2) In the conditional remission of the execution of the remainder of the sentence of prohibition of participation in public events, the court shall determine a probationary period of up to five years, but not shorter than the remainder of the sentence; the probationary period shall begin to run on the day following the day on which the decision on this remission became final. |
|---|
|  | (3) The court may impose on a convicted person from whom the court has conditionally remitted the execution of the remainder of the sentence of prohibition of participation in public events appropriate restrictions and obligations referred to in § 51(3) and (4), aimed at ensuring that he or she leads a proper life. |
|---|
|

| § 72b |

|---|
|  | (1) If a convicted person, from whom the remainder of the sentence of prohibition of participation in public events has been conditionally remitted, has led a proper life during the probationary period and has fulfilled the imposed restrictions and obligations, the court shall declare that he or she has proved himself or herself; otherwise it shall decide, possibly already during the probationary period, that the remainder of the sentence shall be executed. |
|---|
|  | (2) If the court has declared that the conditionally released person has proved himself or herself, the sentence shall be deemed to have been executed on the day on which the decision on remission of the execution of the remainder of the sentence of prohibition of participation in public events became final. |
|---|
|  | (3) Likewise, the sentence of prohibition of participation in public events shall be deemed to have been executed on the day on which the decision on conditional remission of the execution of the remainder of the sentence of prohibition of participation in public events became final, if the court, through no fault of the convicted person, has not ordered within one year from the expiry of the probationary period that the convicted person shall execute the remainder of the sentence. |
|---|
|  | Part Five |
|---|
|  | Protective Measures |
|---|
|  | Protective Treatment |
|---|
|

| § 73 |

|---|
|  | (1) The court shall impose protective treatment in the cases referred to in § 39(2)(c) and § 40(1)(c) or if the perpetrator of an otherwise criminal act is not criminally liable due to insanity and his or her presence at liberty is dangerous. |
|---|
|  | (2) The court may do so also if the perpetrator has committed a criminal offence |
|---|
|  | a) in a state of diminished sanity and his or her presence at liberty is dangerous, |
|---|
|  | b) in a state induced by a mental disorder and his or her presence at liberty is dangerous, |
|---|
|  | c) of a violent nature against a close person or an entrusted person and, having regard to the person of the perpetrator, it can be reasonably assumed that he or she will continue in violent conduct, or |
|---|
|  | d) under the influence of an addictive substance or in connection with its use. |
|---|
|  | (3) Protective treatment under paragraph 2(d) shall be imposed by the court on the perpetrator of a misdemeanour under § 171(1) or (2) only if the perpetrator consents to its imposition. |
|---|
|  | (4) Protective treatment shall not be imposed if, having regard to the person of the perpetrator, it is clear that its purpose cannot be achieved. |
|---|
|  | (5) Protective treatment may be imposed by the court also in addition to a sentence or upon waiver of punishment. |
|---|
|

| § 74 |

|---|
|  | (1) If protective treatment is imposed in addition to an unconditional sentence of imprisonment, its execution shall as a rule begin after commencement of the execution of the sentence in an institution for the execution of sentences. In other cases, protective treatment shall as a rule be executed in an institutional health care facility. If, having regard to the nature of the disease and the treatment options, it can be expected that outpatient treatment will also fulfil the purpose, the court may order this method of treatment as well, or change institutional treatment to outpatient treatment or, in a justified case, vice versa. If the length of execution of the sentence of imprisonment in an institution for the execution of sentences is not sufficient to fulfil the purpose of protective treatment, the court may decide on its continuation in a treatment or outpatient facility. |
|---|
|  | (2) Protective treatment shall last as long as its purpose requires. However, the duration of protective treatment imposed on a perpetrator who uses an addictive substance and has committed a criminal offence under its influence or in connection with its use may be terminated if it is established during its execution that its purpose cannot be achieved. The court shall decide on release from protective treatment. |
|---|
|  | (3) The court shall waive the execution of protective treatment if the circumstances for which it was imposed cease to exist before its commencement. |
|---|
|

| § 75 |

|---|
|  | Protective Education |
|---|
|  | The conditions for imposing and executing protective education are regulated in special provisions on the prosecution of juveniles. |
|---|
|  | Protective Supervision |
|---|
|

| § 76 |

|---|
|  | (1) The court shall impose protective supervision on a perpetrator whom it sentences for a particularly serious felony to an unconditional sentence of imprisonment. |
|---|
|  | (2) The court may also impose protective supervision on a perpetrator of an intentional criminal offence who has already been at least twice in the past in the execution of a sentence of imprisonment for such a criminal offence and to whom it is again imposing an unconditional sentence of imprisonment, if, having regard to the person of the perpetrator, in particular taking into account his or her previous way of life, the environment in which he or she lives, and the nature of the criminal activity committed, it cannot be expected that after the execution of the sentence he or she will lead a proper life. |
|---|
|  | (3) The court may, on the motion of the prosecutor or the director of the institution for the execution of sentences, impose protective supervision on a convicted person before the end of the execution of the sentence of imprisonment even without the fulfilment of the conditions referred to in paragraph 2, if, having regard to the conduct during the execution of the sentence of imprisonment to date, it cannot be expected that after release from the execution of the sentence he or she will lead a proper life. |
|---|
|  | (4) Protective supervision may not be imposed on a juvenile and a convicted person who has been sentenced to life imprisonment. |
|---|
|

| § 77 |

|---|
|  | (1) A convicted person to whom the court has imposed protective supervision is obliged, after release from the execution of the sentence of imprisonment, to report within two working days after release from the execution of the sentence of imprisonment to the probation and mediation officer of the district court in whose district he or she has his or her residence, and within the framework of the imposed protective supervision |
|---|
|  | a) to report the necessary data on the manner and sources of his or her livelihood and also to prove them, |
|---|
|  | b) to report in person at specified intervals, and |
|---|
|  | c) to give advance notice of absence from the place of residence; this does not apply if it concerns regularly recurring absences of which the probation and mediation officer has been informed in advance. |
|---|
|  | (2) The court may also impose on a convicted person to whom protective supervision has been imposed other restrictions or obligations referred to in § 51(3) and (4). |
|---|
|  | (3) A convicted person to whom the court has imposed other restrictions or obligations under paragraph 2 is obliged to submit to control by technical means if such control is ordered. Control by technical means may be ordered if the conditions under a special regulation are met. |
|---|
|

| § 78 |

|---|
|  | (1) Protective supervision shall be imposed for one year to three years. |
|---|
|  | (2) If protective supervision is imposed repeatedly, before the previously imposed protective supervision has been executed, the time for which it is imposed repeatedly, together with the remainder not yet executed of the previously imposed protective supervision, must not exceed five years. |
|---|
|

| § 79 |

|---|
|  | Protective supervision terminates |
|---|
|  | a) upon expiry of the time for which it was imposed, or |
|---|
|  | b) on the day on which the decision that the conditionally released person has proven himself became final. |
|---|
|

| § 80 |

|---|
|  | The court may waive the execution of the remainder of protective supervision if it does not consider its further execution necessary. |
|---|
|  | Detention |
|---|
|

| § 81 |

|---|
|  | (1) If a mental disorder is identified in a convicted person serving a custodial sentence, which according to a professional medical opinion is incurable and his stay at liberty is dangerous to society also taking into account the criminal activity committed, the court shall, upon a motion by the prosecutor or the director of the institution for the execution of sentences, interrupt the execution of the custodial sentence and order his placement in a detention institution. |
|---|
|  | (2) The court may, before the end of the execution of a custodial sentence, decide on placement in a detention institution also of such a perpetrator of an intentional criminal offence who refuses to undergo protective treatment or in whom protective treatment does not fulfil its purpose due to the negative attitude of the patient and whose stay at liberty is dangerous to society; the perpetrator shall be placed in a detention institution after the execution of the custodial sentence. |
|---|
|  | (3) If the court considers it necessary, it may, before the end of the execution of a custodial sentence, decide on placement in a detention institution also of a perpetrator of a felony committed from a sexual motive or a perpetrator who repeatedly commits a particularly serious felony; the perpetrator shall be placed in a detention institution after the execution of the custodial sentence. |
|---|
|  | (4) The court may, on the basis of a professional medical opinion, decide on the placement of a perpetrator in a detention institution also when a perpetrator whose stay at liberty is dangerous is undergoing protective treatment in an institutional health care facility and by his conduct endangers the life or health of other persons; the court shall decide upon a motion by the institutional health care facility. |
|---|
|

| § 82 |

|---|
|  | (1) The purpose of placing a perpetrator in a detention institution is to prevent the perpetrator from further committing criminal offences and otherwise punishable acts by means of a special treatment regime and consistent isolation from society. |
|---|
|  | (2) The stay of a perpetrator in a detention institution shall last until the protection of society from the perpetrator can be ensured by milder means. |
|---|
|  | (3) At least once a year and always upon a motion by the detention institution, the court shall review the justification for holding the perpetrator in the detention institution and, on the basis of a professional medical opinion, shall decide on the further continuation of detention or on the release of the perpetrator from the detention institution if the reasons for detention have ceased, and shall decide on the further execution of the sentence. |
|---|
|

| § 83 |

|---|
|  | Forfeiture of a thing |
|---|
|  | (1) If the penalty of confiscation of a thing referred to in § 60 para. 1 has not been imposed, the court shall impose forfeiture of a thing if |
|---|
|  | a) it belongs to a person who cannot be prosecuted or convicted, |
|---|
|  | b) it belongs to a perpetrator from whose punishment the court has waived, or to a perpetrator against whom criminal prosecution has been discontinued, or to a perpetrator against whom criminal prosecution has been conditionally discontinued, or to a perpetrator against whom criminal prosecution has been discontinued due to approval of a settlement, |
|---|
|  | c) it does not belong to the perpetrator and is proceeds from criminal activity, |
|---|
|  | d) it does not belong to the perpetrator and was intended or used to commit a criminal offence, |
|---|
|  | e) it concerns goods without control stamps or without other control technical measures required by a generally binding legal regulation for its marking for tax purposes, |
|---|
|  | f) the circumstances of the case justify the presumption that the thing could be a source of financing terrorism, or |
|---|
|  | g) the safety of people or property, or another similar public interest, so requires. |
|---|
|  | (2) The state shall become the owner of the forfeited thing, unless the court decides otherwise on the basis of a promulgated international treaty by which the Slovak Republic is bound. |
|---|
|  | (3) The provision of paragraph 1 shall not apply if |
|---|
|  | a) the injured party has acquired from the act a claim for compensation for damage, the satisfaction of which would be prevented by forfeiture of the thing, or |
|---|
|  | b) the value of the thing is in manifest disproportion to the degree of seriousness of the misdemeanour. |
|---|
|  | (4) If the thing referred to in paragraph 1 is unattainable or unidentifiable, or is mixed with the property of the perpetrator or with the property of another person acquired in accordance with the law, the court may impose forfeiture of a thing of such value as corresponds to the value of that thing. |
|---|
|

| § 83a |

|---|
|  | Confiscation of Part of Property |
|---|
|  | (1) The court may impose confiscation of part of property on an offender whom it convicts of an intentional criminal offence for which this Act establishes a penalty of imprisonment with an upper limit of the sentence of at least four years or for a criminal offence of unauthorised access to a computer system under § 247, unauthorised interference with a computer system under § 247a, unauthorised interference with computer data under § 247b, production and possession of an access device, password to a computer system or other data under § 247d, manipulation in public procurement and public auction under § 266, acceptance of a bribe under § 328 to 330, bribery under § 332 to 334, indirect corruption under § 336 or dissemination of child pornography under § 369, by which the offender obtained or attempted to obtain for himself or for another a pecuniary advantage and the court considers that a certain part of his property is proceeds from criminal activity on the basis of proving that the value of property which the offender acquired or transferred to another person within a period of at most five years before the commission of such a criminal offence, during its commission or after its commission is grossly disproportionate to the offender's income acquired in accordance with the law or on the basis of other facts justifying such a conclusion. |
|---|
|  | (2) The court may impose confiscation of part of property even if this property could be confiscated under paragraph 1 and if the offender |
|---|
|  | a) transferred or had transferred this property to another person free of charge or under conspicuously advantageous conditions and this person knew or could and should have known that he transferred such property to them in order to avoid a pecuniary penalty under § 56, a penalty of forfeiture of property under § 58, a penalty of forfeiture of a thing under § 60 or the imposition of confiscation of a thing under § 83, or that this property was acquired in violation of the law, |
|---|
|  | b) transferred or had transferred it to a close person, |
|---|
|  | c) transferred or had transferred it to a legal person in which he himself or together with close persons has a majority property participation, a majority share in voting rights or decisive influence on management, and the offender uses such property free of charge or under conspicuously advantageous conditions, or |
|---|
|  | d) acquired it into the undivided joint ownership of spouses or this property was in the undivided joint ownership of spouses which was settled by agreement and belongs to the offender's spouse. |
|---|
|  | (3) The court is obliged when imposing confiscation of part of property to determine the things which are subject to confiscation. If a gross disproportion between the value of property and the offender's income acquired in accordance with the law in the monitored period has been proven, the court may determine any things belonging to the offender in a value up to the amount of the proven gross disproportion. |
|---|
|  | (4) If the court does not know where the things which are the subject of confiscation of part of property are located, their confiscation is not appropriate with regard to the rights of third parties, or if the person from whom the thing could be confiscated destroys, damages, alienates it or otherwise frustrates its confiscation before the decision on confiscation of part of property is issued, the court may impose confiscation of a thing of such value or a monetary amount which corresponds to the value of this thing. |
|---|
|  | (5) Confiscation of part of property also affects the fruits and benefits of a thing which belong to the person from whom such thing was confiscated. Confiscation of part of property does not apply to things which are necessary for satisfying the living needs of the person from whom part of property is to be confiscated, or of persons whose maintenance and upbringing this person is obliged to provide. |
|---|
|  | (6) The state becomes the owner of the confiscated part of property, unless the court decides otherwise on the basis of a declared international treaty by which the Slovak Republic is bound. |
|---|
|  | THIRD CHAPTER |
|---|
|  | EXTINCTION OF PUNISHABILITY AND PENALTY |
|---|
|

| § 84 |

|---|
|  | Change of Law |
|---|
|  | The punishability of an act which at the time of commission had the characteristics of a criminal offence specified in the special part of this Act ceases if a later law provides that this act is not a criminal offence. |
|---|
|  | Effective Remorse |
|---|
|

| § 85 |

|---|
|  | The punishability of criminal offences of spreading a dangerous human contagious disease under § 163, endangering health by defective food and other items under § 168, hostage-taking under § 185, legalisation of proceeds from criminal activity under § 233, § 234, breach of duty in the administration of another's property under § 238, breach of regulations on the circulation of goods in dealings with foreign countries under § 254, breach of regulations on handling controlled goods and technologies under § 255, § 256, § 257, endangering foreign exchange management under § 258, falsification of data in economic and commercial records under § 260, damaging the financial interests of the European Union under § 263, endangering commercial, banking, postal, telecommunications and tax secrecy under § 264, general endangerment under § 284, damaging and endangering the operation of a generally beneficial facility under § 286, endangering and damaging the environment under § 300, § 301, high treason under § 311, conspiracy against the Slovak Republic under § 312, terror under § 313, § 314, subversion under § 315, § 316, sabotage under § 317, espionage under § 318, endangering a classified fact under § 319, § 320, endangering a confidential fact and a restricted fact under § 353, mutiny of prisoners under § 358, spreading an alarming message under § 361, § 362, genocide under § 418, terrorist attack under § 419, certain forms of participation in terrorism under § 419b, financing of terrorism under § 419c, travel for the purpose of terrorism under § 419d, inhumanity under § 425 and use of a prohibited means of combat and unlawful conduct of combat under § 426 ceases if the offender voluntarily |
|---|
|  | a) prevented or remedied the harmful consequence of the criminal offence, or |
|---|
|  | b) made a report of the criminal offence at a time when the harmful consequence of the criminal offence could still be prevented; the report must be made to a body active in criminal proceedings or to the Police Force, a soldier may also make this report to his superior or service authority and a person serving a sentence of imprisonment or in custody also to a member of the Prison and Court Guard Service. |
|---|
|

| § 86 |

|---|
|  | (1) The punishability of a criminal offence also ceases if it concerns a criminal offence of |
|---|
|  | a) neglect of mandatory maintenance according to § 207, if the criminal offence did not have permanently adverse consequences and the offender subsequently fulfilled his obligation before the court withdrew for final deliberation, |
|---|
|  | b) non-payment of wages and severance pay according to § 214, if the criminal offence did not have permanently adverse consequences and the offender subsequently fulfilled his obligation no later than 60 days from the completion of the criminal offence, |
|---|
|  | c) abuse of participation in economic competition according to § 250, if the offender by his conduct enabled an entrepreneur or other legal person to fulfil the conditions for non-imposition or reduction of a fine according to the law regulating the protection of economic competition, |
|---|
|  | d) tax and insurance premium evasion according to § 276, non-remittance of tax and insurance premium according to § 277 or non-payment of tax and insurance premium according to § 278, if the offender did not commit the criminal offence as a member of an organised group or as a member of a criminal group and if the due tax and its accessories or insurance premium were subsequently paid no later than the following day after the day when the offender, after the completion of his investigation, could become acquainted with its results, |
|---|
|  | e) establishment, organisation and support of a criminal group according to § 296 or establishment, organisation and support of a terrorist group according to § 297, if the offender voluntarily made a notification to an authority active in criminal proceedings or to the Police Force about the criminal group or terrorist group and its activity at a time when the danger that threatened from its further activity could still be removed; a soldier may also make this notification to his superior or service authority and a person serving a custodial sentence or in custody also to a member of the Prison and Court Guard Service, |
|---|
|  | f) bribery according to § 332 or § 333, indirect corruption according to § 336 para. 2 and acceptance and provision of undue advantage according to § 336d, if the offender provided or promised a bribe or undue advantage only because he was requested to do so, and voluntarily made a notification thereof without delay to an authority active in criminal proceedings or to the Police Force; a soldier may also make this notification to his superior or service authority and a person serving a custodial sentence or in custody also to a member of the Prison and Court Guard Service. |
|---|
|  | (2) The punishability of a criminal offence according to paragraph 1 letter d) does not cease if it concerns an offender who was punished for a similar act in the preceding twenty-four months, or if criminal prosecution of the offender for a similar act was discontinued in the preceding twenty-four months due to the cessation of punishability of the criminal offence according to paragraph 1. |
|---|
|  | Limitation of criminal prosecution |
|---|
|

| § 87 |

|---|
|  | (1) The punishability of an act ceases upon expiry of the limitation period, which is |
|---|
|  | a) thirty years, if it concerns a felony for which this Act permits the imposition of a custodial sentence for life, |
|---|
|  | b) twenty years, if it concerns a felony for which this Act in the special part permits the imposition of a custodial sentence with an upper limit of the sentencing range of at least ten years, |
|---|
|  | c) ten years, if it concerns other felonies, |
|---|
|  | d) five years, if it concerns a misdemeanour for which this Act in the special part permits the imposition of a custodial sentence with an upper limit of the sentencing range of at least three years, |
|---|
|  | e) three years for other misdemeanours. |
|---|
|  | (2) The limitation period does not include |
|---|
|  | a) the period during which it was not possible to bring the offender before a court due to a legal obstacle, |
|---|
|  | b) the period during which the offender stayed abroad with the intention of evading criminal prosecution, |
|---|
|  | c) the probationary period of conditional discontinuance of criminal prosecution, |
|---|
|  | d) the period during which the bringing of charges was temporarily postponed, or |
|---|
|  | e) the period during which criminal prosecution was suspended. |
|---|
|  | (3) The limitation of criminal prosecution is interrupted by |
|---|
|  | a) the bringing of charges for the criminal offence whose limitation is concerned, as well as by subsequent acts of an authority active in criminal proceedings, a judge for preparatory proceedings or a court aimed at the criminal prosecution of the offender, or |
|---|
|  | b) if the offender committed an intentional criminal offence during the limitation period. |
|---|
|  | (4) By the interruption of limitation a new limitation period begins. |
|---|
|  | (5) Criminal prosecution becomes time-barred at the earliest fifteen years after the person on whom was committed the criminal offence of unauthorised removal of organs, tissues and cells and unlawful sterilisation according to § 159 para. 2, the criminal offence of trafficking in human beings according to § 179, the criminal offence of rape according to § 199, the criminal offence of sexual violence according to § 200, the criminal offence of sexual abuse according to § 201 to 202, the criminal offence of abuse of a close person and entrusted person according to § 208 and the criminal offence of production of child pornography according to § 368, reached the eighteenth year of age. |
|---|
|

| § 88 |

|---|
|  | Upon expiry of the limitation period the punishability of criminal offences listed in the twelfth chapter of the special part of this Act does not cease, except for the criminal offence of establishment, support and promotion of a movement aimed at suppressing fundamental rights and freedoms according to § 421, the criminal offence of expression of sympathy for a movement aimed at suppressing fundamental rights and freedoms according to § 422, the criminal offence of production of extremist material according to § 422a, the criminal offence of dissemination of extremist material according to § 422b, the criminal offence of possession of extremist material according to § 422c, the criminal offence of denial and approval of the holocaust, crimes of political regimes and crimes against humanity according to § 422d, the criminal offence of defamation of a nation, race and belief according to § 423 and the criminal offence of incitement to national, racial and ethnic hatred according to § 424. |
|---|
|

| § 89 |

|---|
|  | Pardon or mitigation of sentence |
|---|
|  | (1) A sentence shall not be executed if the President of the Slovak Republic, on the basis of his power to pardon sentences, pardoned it to the convicted person. However, this does not apply to other sentences if they were imposed on the offender concurrently and remained unaffected by the decision of the President of the Slovak Republic. |
|---|
|  | (2) If the President of the Slovak Republic, on the basis of his power to mitigate sentences, mitigated the sentence to the convicted person, the sentence thus mitigated shall be executed. However, this does not apply to other sentences if they were imposed on the offender concurrently and remained unaffected by the decision of the President of the Slovak Republic. |
|---|
|  | Limitation of execution of sentence |
|---|
|

| § 90 |

|---|
|  | (1) An imposed sentence cannot be executed after expiry of the limitation period, which is |
|---|
|  | a) twenty years, if it concerns a conviction to a custodial sentence for life, |
|---|
|  | b) fifteen years, if it concerns a conviction to a custodial sentence exceeding ten years, |
|---|
|  | c) ten years, if it concerns a conviction to a custodial sentence of at least five years, |
|---|
|  | d) five years for conviction to another sentence. |
|---|
|  | (2) The limitation period begins with the finality of the judgment and in the case of conditional conviction, conditional conviction with supervision, conditional release or conditional release with supervision with the finality of the decision that the sentence shall be executed. |
|---|
|  | (3) The limitation period does not include the period during which it was not possible to execute the sentence because the convicted person stayed abroad with the intention of evading the sentence, or a custodial sentence was being executed on him. |
|---|
|  | (4) The limitation of execution of sentence is interrupted if |
|---|
|  | a) the court took a measure aimed at the execution of the sentence whose limitation is concerned, or |
|---|
|  | b) the convicted person committed an intentional criminal offence during the limitation period. |
|---|
|  | (5) By the interruption of limitation a new limitation period begins. |
|---|
|

| § 91 |

|---|
|  | The execution of a sentence imposed for criminal offences referred to in the twelfth chapter of the Special Part of this Act is not subject to a statute of limitations, except for the criminal offence of establishing, supporting and promoting a movement aimed at suppressing fundamental rights and freedoms under § 421, the criminal offence of expressing sympathy for a movement aimed at suppressing fundamental rights and freedoms under § 422, the criminal offence of producing extremist material under § 422a, the criminal offence of disseminating extremist material under § 422b, the criminal offence of possessing extremist material under § 422c, the criminal offence of denying and approving the Holocaust, crimes of political regimes and crimes against humanity under § 422d, the criminal offence of defaming a nation, race and belief under § 423 and the criminal offence of inciting national, racial and ethnic hatred under § 424. |
|---|
|  | Expungement of conviction |
|---|
|

| § 92 |

|---|
|  | (1) The court may expunge a conviction if the convicted person has led a proper life after serving or remission of the sentence, or after the statute of limitations for its execution, continuously for a period of at least |
|---|
|  | a) ten years, if it concerns a conviction to a sentence of imprisonment exceeding five years, |
|---|
|  | b) five years, if it concerns a conviction to a sentence of imprisonment exceeding one year, |
|---|
|  | c) three years, if it concerns a conviction to a sentence of imprisonment not exceeding one year. |
|---|
|  | (2) If it concerns a conviction to sentences referred to in § 32 letters b) to l), expungement occurs upon their execution. |
|---|
|  | (3) If the convicted person has demonstrated by his exemplary conduct after serving or remission of the sentence or the statute of limitations for its execution that he has reformed, the court may expunge the conviction at the request of the convicted person even before the expiry of the period referred to in paragraph 1. |
|---|
|  | (4) The period referred to in paragraph 1, in the case where a person conditionally released and a person conditionally released under supervision is deemed to have served the sentence on the day when the convicted person was conditionally released, is governed by the length of the actual execution of the sentence. If the sentence was mitigated by a decision of the President of the Slovak Republic, it is governed by the length of the sentence thus mitigated. |
|---|
|  | (5) A conviction is expunged if so ordered by the President of the Slovak Republic on the basis of his right to expunge convictions in the form of pardon or amnesty. |
|---|
|

| § 93 |

|---|
|  | (1) If a conviction has been expunged, the offender is regarded as if he had not been convicted. |
|---|
|  | (2) If several sentences were imposed on the offender concurrently, the conviction cannot be expunged until the period for expungement of the sentence of imprisonment under § 92 paragraph 1 has elapsed, if one was imposed. |
|---|
|  | FOURTH CHAPTER |
|---|
|  | SPECIAL PROVISIONS ON THE PROSECUTION OF JUVENILES |
|---|
|  | Part One |
|---|
|  | Criminal liability |
|---|
|

| § 94 |

|---|
|  | General provision |
|---|
|  | (1) A person who at the time of committing a criminal offence has reached the age of fourteen and has not exceeded the age of eighteen is considered a juvenile. |
|---|
|  | (2) If this chapter does not contain special provisions, the other provisions of this Act shall apply to a juvenile. |
|---|
|

| § 95 |

|---|
|  | Criminal liability |
|---|
|  | (1) A juvenile under the age of fifteen who at the time of committing the act has not reached such a level of intellectual and moral maturity as to be able to recognise its unlawfulness or control his conduct is not criminally liable for that act. |
|---|
|  | (2) A misdemeanour, the elements of which are set out in this Act, is not a criminal offence if it was committed by a juvenile and if its seriousness is minor. |
|---|
|  | Part Two |
|---|
|  | Extinction of criminal liability |
|---|
|

| § 96 |

|---|
|  | Statute of limitations for criminal prosecution |
|---|
|  | (1) Criminal liability for an act expires upon the expiry of the limitation period, which is |
|---|
|  | a) ten years, if it concerns a criminal offence for which this Act provides a sentence of life imprisonment, |
|---|
|  | b) five years, if the upper limit of the sentence of imprisonment is at least ten years, |
|---|
|  | c) three years for other criminal offences. |
|---|
|  | (2) Criminal liability for those criminal offences where this Act so provides does not expire upon the expiry of the limitation period. |
|---|
|  | Part Three |
|---|
|  | Imposition of sanctions and educational measures on a juvenile |
|---|
|

| § 97 |

|---|
|  | Purpose of sanctions and educational measures |
|---|
|  | (1) The purpose of a sentence for a juvenile is primarily to educate him to be a proper citizen, while the sentence is also to have an effect on preventing unlawful acts and appropriately protecting society; the imposed sentence is also to lead to the restoration of disrupted social relationships and to the integration of the juvenile into the family and social environment. |
|---|
|  | (2) The purpose of a protective measure and an educational measure for a juvenile is to positively influence the mental, moral and social development of the juvenile with regard to the achieved level of his intellectual and moral development, his personal characteristics, family upbringing and the environment from which he comes, and thereby also protect the juvenile from harmful influences and society from the commission of criminal activity. |
|---|
|  | (3) When imposing a sentence, protective measure or educational measure, regard must be had to the personality of the juvenile, his age, intellectual and moral maturity, state of health, his personal, family and social circumstances, and they must be proportionate to the nature and seriousness of the act committed and must lead to the integration of the juvenile into the family and social environment so as to prevent unlawful acts. |
|---|
|  | Waiver of punishment |
|---|
|

| § 98 |

|---|
|  | The court may waive the punishment of a juvenile who has committed a misdemeanour, regrets having committed it and shows an effective effort to reform, if |
|---|
|  | a) having regard to the nature of the act committed and the previous life of the juvenile, it can reasonably be expected that the hearing of the case before the court alone will suffice for his reform, or |
|---|
|  | b) the court accepts a guarantee for the reform of the juvenile and considers that, having regard to the educational influence of the person who offered the guarantee, the nature of the act committed and the person of the juvenile, the imposition of a sentence does not appear necessary. |
|---|
|

| § 99 |

|---|
|  | The court may also waive the punishment of a juvenile for a misdemeanour if |
|---|
|  | a) the juvenile committed the act in a state that was caused by a mental disorder, and the court considers that the protective treatment which it is simultaneously imposing will ensure the reform of the juvenile better than a sentence, or |
|---|
|  | b) a protective measure or educational measures are being carried out in respect of the juvenile and the imposition of a sentence is not necessary to achieve the purpose of the Act. |
|---|
|

| § 100 |

|---|
|  | If the court has waived punishment, the juvenile is regarded as if he had not been convicted. |
|---|
|

| § 101 |

|---|
|  | Conditional Waiver of Punishment |
|---|
|  | (1) Under the conditions set out in § 98, the court may conditionally waive the punishment of a juvenile if it considers it necessary to monitor the juvenile's conduct during a specified period. |
|---|
|  | (2) In the case of a conditional waiver of punishment, the court shall determine a probationary period of up to one year. At the same time, it may impose on the juvenile appropriate restrictions and obligations referred to in § 51(3) and (4), aimed at ensuring that he or she leads a proper life; as a rule, it shall impose on him or her the obligation to compensate, to the best of his or her ability, for the damage caused. |
|---|
|  | (3) The court may, having regard to the circumstances of the case and the person of the juvenile, maintain the conditional waiver of punishment in force notwithstanding that the juvenile has given cause for the imposition of a sentence, and |
|---|
|  | a) establish probation supervision over the juvenile if it has not already been imposed, |
|---|
|  | b) extend the probationary period appropriately, but not by more than one year, or |
|---|
|  | c) establish educational measures aimed at ensuring that he or she leads a proper life. |
|---|
|  | (4) If a juvenile in respect of whom punishment has been conditionally waived has led a proper life during the probationary period and has complied with the conditions imposed, the court shall declare that he or she has proved himself or herself; otherwise it shall decide, possibly already during the probationary period, on the imposition of a sentence. |
|---|
|  | (5) If the court has not made a decision pursuant to paragraph 4 within one year of the expiry of the probationary period without the juvenile being at fault, he or she shall be deemed to have proved himself or herself. |
|---|
|  | (6) If it has been declared that a juvenile in respect of whom punishment has been conditionally waived has proved himself or herself, or if he or she is deemed to have proved himself or herself, he or she shall be regarded as if he or she had not been convicted. |
|---|
|  | Part Four |
|---|
|  | Protective Education |
|---|
|

| § 102 |

|---|
|  | Grounds for Imposing Protective Education |
|---|
|  | (1) If the court convicts a juvenile, it may impose protective education on him or her if |
|---|
|  | a) the juvenile's upbringing is not properly provided for and this deficiency cannot be remedied in the family in which he or she lives, |
|---|
|  | b) the juvenile's upbringing to date has been neglected, or |
|---|
|  | c) the environment in which the juvenile lives does not provide a guarantee of his or her proper upbringing. |
|---|
|  | (2) Protective education may not be imposed if the person concerned is a soldier. |
|---|
|

| § 103 |

|---|
|  | Implementation of Protective Education |
|---|
|  | (1) Protective education shall be carried out in special educational facilities (hereinafter "protective institutional education") or may also be carried out in a professional substitute family (hereinafter "protective family education"); however, if the health condition of the juvenile so requires, it shall be carried out preferentially in a facility for institutional health care. |
|---|
|  | (2) Protective education shall last as long as its purpose requires, but at most until the juvenile reaches the age of eighteen; if the interests of the juvenile so require, the court may extend it until he or she reaches the age of nineteen. |
|---|
|  | (3) If protective education cannot be carried out immediately, the court shall order supervision by a probation and mediation officer for the period until its commencement. |
|---|
|  | (4) The court shall waive the implementation of protective education if the circumstances for which it was imposed cease to exist before its commencement. |
|---|
|  | (5) If the re-education of the juvenile has progressed to such an extent that it can be expected that even without the restrictions to which he or she is subject in the educational facility or in the designated family, he or she will behave properly, but all the circumstances for which protective education was imposed have not yet ceased to exist, the court may conditionally waive protective education or conditionally place the juvenile outside the educational facility or substitute family. In doing so, it may impose on the juvenile supervision by a probation and mediation officer or another educational measure. |
|---|
|  | (6) If the juvenile fails to fulfil the expectation that, without the restrictions to which he or she is subject within the framework of the designated protective education, he or she will behave properly, the court shall revoke the conditional waiver of protective education or the conditional placement outside the educational facility or designated family and decide that the implementation of protective education shall continue. |
|---|
|  | Change in the Method of Implementation of Protective Education |
|---|
|

| § 104 |

|---|
|  | (1) Protective institutional education may be changed to protective family education at any time and vice versa. |
|---|
|  | (2) If necessary having regard to the purpose of protective education, the natural person to whom the juvenile has been entrusted for protective family education may also be changed. |
|---|
|

| § 105 |

|---|
|  | If a person who has reached the age of twelve and is under the age of fourteen has committed an act for which this Act permits the imposition of a sentence of life imprisonment, the court shall impose protective education on him or her in civil proceedings, also on the motion of the prosecutor; the court may also do so where it is necessary to ensure the proper upbringing of a person under the age of fourteen who has committed an act which would otherwise be a criminal offence, or of a person under the age of fifteen who has committed the criminal offence of sexual abuse under § 201. |
|---|
|  | Part Five |
|---|
|  | Educational Measures |
|---|
|

| § 106 |

|---|
|  | Types of Educational Measures and Their Imposition |
|---|
|  | (1) In the case of a conditional waiver of punishment or in preparatory proceedings, educational measures may be imposed on a juvenile in order to achieve the purpose of the Act. |
|---|
|  | (2) Educational measures are: |
|---|
|  | a) educational obligations and restrictions, |
|---|
|  | b) admonition with warning. |
|---|
|  | (3) Educational measures may be imposed by the court and, in preparatory proceedings, with the consent of the person against whom the proceedings are being conducted, also by the prosecutor. The juvenile may at any time during criminal prosecution until the end of the preparatory proceedings revoke his or her consent by a written declaration addressed to the prosecutor; the implementation of the educational measure thereby ends. |
|---|
|  | (4) In the event that it is proved that complete or timely fulfilment of the educational measure is impossible for the juvenile or that he or she cannot fulfil it for other serious reasons or culpably fails to fulfil it, the court and, in preparatory proceedings, the prosecutor shall revoke or modify the imposed educational measure. |
|---|
|

| § 107 |

|---|
|  | Educational Obligations and Restrictions |
|---|
|  | (1) The court and, in preparatory proceedings, the prosecutor may impose on the juvenile educational obligations and restrictions, in particular that he or she |
|---|
|  | a) submit to probation supervision carried out by a probation and mediation officer, |
|---|
|  | b) live with a parent or with another adult who is responsible for his or her upbringing, |
|---|
|  | c) endeavour to reach a settlement with the injured party, |
|---|
|  | d) compensate, to the best of his or her ability, for the damage caused by the criminal offence or otherwise contribute to the removal of the consequences of the criminal offence, |
|---|
|  | e) perform socially beneficial activities free of charge in his or her free time, |
|---|
|  | f) undergo treatment for harmful addiction, |
|---|
|  | g) undergo in his or her free time a programme of social training, psychological counselling, psychotherapeutic, educational, further training, retraining or other suitable programme for the development of his or her social skills and the personality of the juvenile, which is not a probation programme. |
|---|
|  | (2) The performance of socially beneficial activities of a certain type may be imposed on a juvenile only in such a way that it does not disrupt his or her preparation for a future profession, in particular the fulfilment of obligations related to the educational programme of a school, or the exercise of a profession or employment, and may involve a maximum of four hours per day, eighteen hours per week, sixty hours in total. |
|---|
|

| § 108 |

|---|
|  | Admonition with Warning |
|---|
|  | By admonition with warning, the court and, in preparatory proceedings, the prosecutor shall emphatically reproach the juvenile in the presence of his or her legal representative for the unlawfulness of his or her act and warn him or her of the sanctions that threaten him or her under this Act if he or she were to commit criminal activity in the future. |
|---|
|  | Part Six |
|---|
|  | Sentences |
|---|
|

| § 109 |

|---|
|  | Types of Punishments |
|---|
|  | The court may impose on a juvenile only |
|---|
|  | a) community service punishment, |
|---|
