|  | b) monetary punishment, |
|---|
|  | c) punishment of forfeiture of a thing, |
|---|
|  | d) punishment of prohibition of activity, |
|---|
|  | e) punishment of prohibition of attendance at public events, |
|---|
|  | f) punishment of expulsion, |
|---|
|  | g) punishment of house arrest, |
|---|
|  | h) punishment of imprisonment. |
|---|
|

| § 110 |

|---|
|  | Imposition of Punishment |
|---|
|  | (1) When determining the punishment, the court shall also take into account as a mitigating circumstance how the juvenile fulfilled the imposed educational obligations and restrictions referred to in § 107. |
|---|
|  | (2) If the court imposes a cumulative punishment or an aggregate punishment on an offender who committed criminal offences partly before reaching the age of eighteen and partly after reaching that age, it shall proceed according to § 41 and § 42, whereby the limits of the sentencing ranges for criminal offences committed before reaching the age of eighteen shall be assessed according to § 117. |
|---|
|

| § 111 |

|---|
|  | Community Service Punishment |
|---|
|  | When imposing community service punishment, the upper limit of this punishment must not exceed half of the upper limit of the sentencing range established by law. Community service punishment must not, by its nature or the circumstances under which it is performed, endanger the health, safety or moral development of juveniles. |
|---|
|

| § 112 |

|---|
|  | Punishment of Prohibition of Activity |
|---|
|  | (1) The court may impose a prohibition of activity on a juvenile only if it does not obstruct preparation for his or her profession, and the upper limit of this punishment must not exceed five years. |
|---|
|  | (2) If the court convicts a juvenile under the conditions of § 61 para. 3, it shall impose the punishment of prohibition of activity in the upper half of the sentencing range referred to in paragraph 1. |
|---|
|  | (3) If the court convicts a juvenile under the conditions of § 61 para. 4, it shall impose the punishment of prohibition of activity for seven to fifteen years. |
|---|
|  | (4) If the court convicts a juvenile under the conditions of § 61 para. 5, it shall impose the punishment of prohibition of activity for seven to fifteen years. |
|---|
|

| § 112a |

|---|
|  | Punishment of Prohibition of Attendance at Public Events |
|---|
|  | The court may impose the punishment of prohibition of attendance at public events on a juvenile only under the conditions established by this Act, for a term of up to five years. |
|---|
|

| § 113 |

|---|
|  | Punishment of Expulsion |
|---|
|  | The court may impose expulsion on a juvenile only under the conditions established by this Act, for a term of one to five years. In doing so, it shall also take into account the family and personal circumstances of the juvenile, bearing in mind that the juvenile should not be exposed to the danger of moral decline by this punishment. |
|---|
|

| § 114 |

|---|
|  | Monetary Punishment |
|---|
|  | (1) The court may impose a monetary punishment in the amount of EUR 30 to EUR 16,590 if the conditions established by this Act are met, if the juvenile is gainfully employed or his or her financial circumstances permit the imposition of this punishment. |
|---|
|  | (2) If the court imposes a monetary punishment on a juvenile, it shall determine, in the event that its execution could be intentionally frustrated within the prescribed period, a substitute punishment of imprisonment of up to one year. However, the substitute punishment of imprisonment must not, even together with the imposed punishment of imprisonment, exceed the upper limit of the sentencing range reduced according to § 117 para. 1. |
|---|
|  | (3) After the decision imposing a monetary punishment on a juvenile becomes final, the court may decide, after hearing the juvenile, that its payment or the unexecuted remainder shall be replaced by the convicted juvenile performing community service within a probation programme. |
|---|
|

| § 115 |

|---|
|  | Conditional Suspension of Execution of Monetary Punishment |
|---|
|  | (1) The court may conditionally suspend the execution of a monetary punishment if |
|---|
|  | a) having regard to the person of the juvenile, in particular taking into account his or her previous life and the environment in which he or she lives and works, and the circumstances of the case, it considers that the purpose of the punishment will be achieved even without its execution, or |
|---|
|  | b) it accepts a guarantee for the rehabilitation of the juvenile and, having regard to the educational influence of the person offering the guarantee, it considers that the purpose of the punishment will be achieved even without its execution. |
|---|
|  | (2) The granting of conditional suspension of a monetary punishment does not affect the execution of other punishments imposed alongside the monetary punishment, unless the court decides otherwise. |
|---|
|

| § 116 |

|---|
|  | Probationary Period |
|---|
|  | (1) In the case of conditional suspension of execution of a monetary punishment, the court shall determine a probationary period of up to three years. At the same time, it may impose on the juvenile appropriate restrictions and obligations referred to in § 51 para. 3 and 4, aimed at ensuring that he or she leads a proper life, and the obligation to submit to monitoring by technical means if such monitoring is ordered; the probationary period begins to run on the day following the day on which the judgment becomes final. Monitoring by technical means may be ordered if the conditions under a special regulation are met. |
|---|
|  | (2) The period during which the conditionally convicted person led a proper life during the probationary period and complied with the imposed conditions shall be counted towards the probationary period newly determined in conditional conviction for the same act or towards the probationary period determined when imposing a cumulative punishment or an aggregate punishment. |
|---|
|  | (3) The provisions on conditional conviction shall apply to decisions on proving oneself during the probationary period. |
|---|
|

| § 116a |

|---|
|  | Punishment of House Arrest |
|---|
|  | (1) The court may impose the punishment of house arrest on a juvenile if the conditions established by this Act are met and with the consent of his or her legal representative, for up to one year. |
|---|
|  | (2) The court may impose on the juvenile during the execution of the punishment of house arrest the educational measures referred to in § 107 para. 1, aimed at ensuring that he or she leads a proper life. |
|---|
|

| § 117 |

|---|
|  | Punishment of Imprisonment |
|---|
|  | (1) The sentencing ranges of imprisonment established in this Act are reduced by half for juveniles; the upper limit of the reduced sentencing range must not exceed seven years and the lower limit of the reduced sentencing range two years. |
|---|
|  | (2) The court may impose an unconditional punishment of imprisonment on a juvenile only on condition that, having regard to the circumstances of the case, the person of the juvenile or the measures previously imposed, the imposition of another punishment would manifestly not lead to the achievement of the purpose of punishment under this Act. |
|---|
|  | (3) In the event that a juvenile has committed a particularly serious felony and the degree of seriousness of such a particularly serious felony for society is exceptionally high having regard to the reprehensible manner of commission of the act or having regard to the reprehensible motive or having regard to the serious and difficult-to-remedy consequence, the court may impose a punishment of imprisonment of more than seven years up to fifteen years if it considers that the punishment referred to in paragraph 1 is insufficient to achieve the purpose. |
|---|
|  | (4) The punishment of imprisonment for persons who have not reached the age of eighteen is executed in institutions for the execution of punishment for juveniles. |
|---|
|  | (5) The court may decide that the punishment shall also be executed in an institution for the execution of punishment for juveniles in the case of a juvenile who has already reached the age of eighteen; in doing so, it shall take into account in particular the length of the punishment and the degree and nature of the juvenile's disturbance. |
|---|
|

| § 118 |

|---|
|  | Extraordinary Reduction of Punishment of Imprisonment |
|---|
|  | (1) If the court finds that the conditions of § 39 para. 1 or 2 for extraordinary reduction of the punishment of imprisonment below the lower limit of the sentencing range are met, it is not bound in the case of a juvenile by the limitations under § 39 para. 3. |
|---|
|  | (2) The provision of paragraph 1 shall not apply if it concerns a criminal offence for which a punishment of life imprisonment may be imposed under this Act. |
|---|
|

| § 119 |

|---|
|  | Conditional Suspension of Sentence Execution and Conditional Suspension of Sentence Execution with Probation Supervision |
|---|
|  | (1) If the court conditionally suspends the execution of a custodial sentence for a juvenile for a probationary period or conditionally suspends the custodial sentence for a probationary period with probation supervision, it shall simultaneously determine the probationary period to be one to three years. The juvenile is obliged to submit to monitoring by technical means if such monitoring is ordered. Monitoring by technical means may be ordered if the conditions under a special regulation are met. |
|---|
|  | (2) The court may, having regard to the circumstances of the case and the person of the juvenile, maintain the conditional suspension of sentence execution or the conditional suspension of sentence execution with probation supervision notwithstanding that the juvenile has given cause for ordering the execution of the custodial sentence, and |
|---|
|  | a) establish probation supervision over the juvenile if it has not already been imposed, |
|---|
|  | b) appropriately extend the probationary period, but not by more than two years, provided that it does not exceed the upper limit of the probationary period of five years, |
|---|
|  | c) impose a further educational measure referred to in § 106(2), aimed at ensuring that he leads a proper life, or |
|---|
|  | d) order monitoring of the imposed educational restrictions or obligations by technical means if the conditions under a special regulation are met and if such monitoring has not been ordered to date. |
|---|
|

| § 120 |

|---|
|  | Limitation of Sentence Execution |
|---|
|  | (1) An imposed sentence cannot be executed after the expiry of the limitation period, which is |
|---|
|  | a) ten years if a custodial sentence was imposed under § 117(3), or |
|---|
|  | b) three years for other criminal offences. |
|---|
|  | (2) The execution of a sentence imposed for criminal offences referred to in the twelfth chapter of the Special Part of this Act is not subject to limitation even in the case of a juvenile, except for the criminal offence of establishing, supporting and promoting a movement aimed at suppressing fundamental rights and freedoms under § 421, the criminal offence of expressing sympathy for a movement aimed at suppressing fundamental rights and freedoms under § 422, the criminal offence of producing extremist material under § 422a, the criminal offence of disseminating extremist material under § 422b, the criminal offence of possessing extremist material under § 422c, the criminal offence of denying and approving the holocaust, crimes of political regimes and crimes against humanity under § 422d, the criminal offence of defaming a nation, race and belief under § 423 and the criminal offence of incitement to national, racial and ethnic hatred under § 424. |
|---|
|

| § 121 |

|---|
|  | Expungement of Conviction |
|---|
|  | (1) A juvenile who has been imposed or by a decision of the President of the Slovak Republic has had mitigated or remitted a custodial sentence not exceeding one year in length shall be regarded as if he had not been convicted, from the date of execution of that sentence or from the date on which such sentence or the remainder thereof was finally waived. |
|---|
|  | (2) The court shall decide whether the conviction of a juvenile to a custodial sentence to which paragraph 1 does not apply is expunged, having regard to the juvenile's conduct during the execution of the custodial sentence, after the execution of that sentence. If the sentence has been mitigated by a decision of the President of the Slovak Republic, the court shall proceed in this manner after the juvenile has been released after serving the mitigated sentence. |
|---|
|  | (3) If the court has declared that a juvenile conditionally sentenced to a custodial sentence, conditionally sentenced to a custodial sentence with probation supervision or conditionally released from the execution of a custodial sentence has proved himself, he shall be regarded as if he had not been convicted. |
|---|
|  | (4) A juvenile who has been imposed a pecuniary penalty shall be regarded as if he had not been convicted as soon as the sentence has been executed or the execution of the sentence or the remainder thereof has been finally waived. |
|---|
|  | (5) A juvenile who has been imposed a sentence of forfeiture of property shall be regarded as if he had not been convicted as soon as the sentence has been executed. |
|---|
|  | (6) A juvenile who has been imposed a sentence of community service or a sentence of prohibition of activity shall be regarded as if he had not been convicted as soon as the sentence has been executed or its execution or the remainder thereof has been finally waived. |
|---|
|  | (7) A juvenile who has been imposed a sentence of expulsion shall be regarded as if he had not been convicted as soon as the period for which it was imposed has expired, if he has duly served the sentence. |
|---|
|  | (8) A juvenile who has been imposed a sentence of prohibition of attendance at public events shall be regarded as if he had not been convicted as soon as the sentence has been executed. |
|---|
|  | PART FIVE |
|---|
|  | INTERPRETATION OF TERMS |
|---|
|  | Division One |
|---|
|  | General Terms |
|---|
|

| § 122 |

|---|
|  | Criminal Offense and Manners of Its Commission |
|---|
|  | (1) An act also means an omission of such conduct to which the offender was obliged according to the circumstances and his conditions. |
|---|
|  | (2) A criminal offense is committed publicly if it is committed |
|---|
|  | a) by the content of a printed matter or by dissemination of a document, by film, radio, television, use of a computer network or by another similarly effective manner, or |
|---|
|  | b) before more than two persons simultaneously present. |
|---|
|  | (3) A criminal offense is committed with a weapon if the offender or, with his knowledge, any of the co-perpetrators uses a weapon for attack, to overcome or prevent resistance, or has it on his person for such purpose; a weapon means, unless otherwise provided by an individual provision, any thing by which an attack against the body can be made more forceful. A criminal offense is committed with a weapon also when the offender uses an imitation of a weapon or has it on his person with the intent that it be considered genuine. |
|---|
|  | (4) A criminal offense is committed by breaking in if the offender entered an enclosed space by unlawful overcoming of a lock or by overcoming another security obstacle using force or deceit. |
|---|
|  | (5) A criminal offense is committed in a dwelling if it is committed in another person's house or apartment or in other premises serving for habitation, including premises and land belonging to them, if they are enclosed as part of the dwelling. |
|---|
|  | (6) A criminal offense is committed by deceit if it was committed by exploiting an error which the offender caused, or by using a ruse. |
|---|
|  | (7) A criminal offense is committed by violence if the offender uses physical violence against the bodily integrity of another person for its commission, or if it is committed on a person whom the offender rendered defenseless by deceit, or if the offender used violence against another's property. |
|---|
|  | (8) A criminal offense is committed using coercion when the offender uses psychological violence against another for its commission. |
|---|
|  | (9) A criminal offense is committed repeatedly if the offender successively committed several identical criminal offenses by repeated separate acts among which there is no objective or subjective connection, whereby the punishability of each of them is assessed separately. |
|---|
|  | (10) A continuing criminal offense is considered to be when the offender continued in committing the same criminal offense. The punishability of all partial attacks is assessed as one criminal offense if all partial attacks of the same offender are connected by an objective connection in time, manner of their commission and in the object of attack, as well as a subjective connection, in particular a unifying intent of the offender to commit the said criminal offense. |
|---|
|  | (11) A collective criminal offense is considered to be its commission by several acts which separately are not yet a criminal offense. The punishability of all such acts is assessed jointly. |
|---|
|  | (12) A continuous criminal offense is considered to be the causing and maintaining or only maintaining of an unlawful state. |
|---|
|  | (13) If the accused continues in the conduct for which he is prosecuted even after notification of the bringing of charges, such conduct is assessed from this procedural act as a new fact; this does not apply if it concerns the criminal offense of neglect of mandatory maintenance under § 207. In such case it is a continuation of conduct until the time when the judgment of the court of first instance is pronounced or until the court of second instance has retired for final deliberation. |
|---|
|  | (14) A criminal offense is committed in connection with participation in a public event if it is committed by a participant of a public event at the time and place of the public event or in its vicinity or at another place during movement to the place of the public event or from the place of the public event, including means of transport or service establishments. A public event is considered to be a public assembly, cultural, sports and other event accessible to the public. |
|---|
|

| § 123 |

|---|
|  | Harm to Health |
|---|
|  | (1) Harm to health for the purposes of this Act means any damage to the health of another. |
|---|
|  | (2) Bodily harm for the purposes of this Act means such damage to the health of another which objectively required medical examination, treatment or cure, during which the usual way of life of the injured person was impaired not only for a short time. |
|---|
|  | (3) Serious harm to health for the purposes of this Act means only a serious health disorder or serious illness, which is |
|---|
|  | a) mutilation, |
|---|
|  | b) loss or substantial reduction of working capacity, |
|---|
|  | c) paralysis of a limb, |
|---|
|  | d) loss or substantial weakening of the function of a sensory organ, |
|---|
|  | e) damage to an important organ, |
|---|
|  | f) disfigurement, |
|---|
|  | g) causing a miscarriage or killing a fetus, |
|---|
|  | h) agonizing suffering, or |
|---|
|  | i) a health disorder lasting a longer time. |
|---|
|  | (4) A health disorder lasting a longer time for the purposes of this Act means a disorder which objectively required treatment, possibly also incapacity for work, lasting at least forty-two calendar days, during which it seriously affected the usual way of life of the injured person. |
|---|
|  | Damage |
|---|
|

| § 124 |

|---|
|  | (1) Damage for the purposes of this Act means harm to property or a real loss of property or rights of the injured person or his other harm which is in causal connection with the criminal offense, regardless of whether it is damage to a thing or to rights. Damage for the purposes of this Act also means obtaining a benefit in causal connection with a criminal offense. |
|---|
|  | (2) Damage in the sense of paragraph 1 also means harm to profit to which the injured person would otherwise be entitled given the circumstances and his conditions or which he could justifiably achieve. |
|---|
|  | (3) In criminal offenses against the environment, damage means the sum of ecological harm and property damage, whereby property damage includes also the costs of restoring the environment to its previous state. In the criminal offense of unauthorized handling of waste under § 302, the extent of the act means the price at which waste is usually collected, transported, exported, imported, recovered, disposed of or deposited at the time and place of detection of the act, and the price for removing waste from a place not designated for its deposit. |
|---|
|

| § 125 |

|---|
|  | (1) Minor damage means damage exceeding the amount of 266 euros. Greater damage means an amount reaching at least ten times such amount. Substantial damage means an amount reaching at least one hundred times such amount. Damage of large extent means an amount reaching at least five hundred times such amount. These criteria shall be applied equally to determining the amount of benefit, value of a thing and extent of an act. |
|---|
|  | (2) If this Act in the special part requires in the basic factual basis the causing of damage as a property consequence of a criminal offense and does not state its amount, it is understood that at least minor damage must be caused. |
|---|
|

| § 126 |

|---|
|  | (1) When determining the amount of damage, the basis is the price at which the thing that was the subject of the attack is usually sold at the time and place of the act. If the amount of damage cannot be determined in this way, the basis is the costs reasonably incurred to acquire the same or similar thing or to restore the thing to its previous state. |
|---|
|  | (2) In the case of harm to the environment, harm caused to protected species of animals and plants, specimens or trees, or damage to monument-protected things or things having historical, artistic or scientific value, when determining the harm or amount of damage, the basis shall also include the value of the thing determined by law or other generally binding legal regulation issued on the basis of law. |
|---|
|  | (3) If the amount of damage or harm cannot be determined by any of the methods under paragraphs 1 and 2, or if there are serious doubts about the correctness of the amount of damage or harm thus determined, its amount shall be determined on the basis of an expert opinion or confirmation of a legal person whose competence or subject of activity provides a guarantee of objectivity in determining the damage or harm; otherwise the amount of damage shall be determined on the basis of an expert report. |
|---|
|  | Person |
|---|
|

| § 127 |

|---|
|  | (1) A child means a person younger than eighteen years, unless this Act provides otherwise. |
|---|
|  | (2) A person close to the age of juveniles means a person who has reached the eighteenth year of age and has not exceeded twenty-one years of age. |
|---|
|  | (3) An elderly person for the purposes of this Act means a person older than sixty years. |
|---|
|  | (4) A close person for the purposes of this Act means a relative in direct line, adopter, adoptee, sibling and spouse; other persons in a family or similar relationship shall be considered close persons to each other only if the harm suffered by one of them would be rightfully felt by the other as their own harm. |
|---|
|  | (5) A close person for the purposes of the criminal offences of extortion under § 189, rape under § 199(2), sexual violence under § 200(2), sexual abuse under § 201(2), abuse of a close person and entrusted person under § 208, dangerous threatening under § 360(2), dangerous stalking under § 360a, dangerous electronic harassment under § 360b or financing of terrorism under § 419c(2) also means a former spouse, partner, former partner, parent of a common child and a person who is a close person to them under paragraph 4, as well as a person who lives or lived with the perpetrator in a common household. |
|---|
|  | (6) A sick person for the purposes of this Act means a person who at the time of the act suffers from a physical illness or mental illness, even if temporary, regardless of whether they are temporarily incapacitated for work, as well as a person with altered working capacity, a disabled person or a person with severe health impairment, where the intensity of such illness or impairment corresponds to serious harm to health. |
|---|
|  | (7) A defenceless person for the purposes of this Act means a person who, given their age, state of health, circumstances of the act or circumstances on the part of the perpetrator, had no hope of effectively defending themselves against their attack. |
|---|
|  | (8) A person entrusted to care or supervision for the purposes of this Act means a person who, given their age or state of health or for another reason, is entrusted to another on the basis of a court decision or other state authority decision, on the basis of a contract, so that they supervise them, care for them, provide for them or educate them in their household or in a facility designated for this purpose or elsewhere. |
|---|
|  | (9) A dependent person for the purposes of this Act means a person who is dependent on the perpetrator for their subsistence, upbringing, material or other provision or care. |
|---|
|  | (10) An addicted person means a person addicted to addictive substances or to addictive harmful activities. |
|---|
|  | (11) A subordinate person is a person who, as a result of their position, is personally, in terms of work, service or otherwise by their position, job classification, function or rank subordinate to the perpetrator and as a result is obliged to receive and fulfil their instructions, orders or commands. |
|---|
|  | (12) Several persons for the purposes of this Act means at least three persons. |
|---|
|

| § 128 |

|---|
|  | (1) For the purposes of this Act, a public official means the President of the Slovak Republic, a Member of the National Council of the Slovak Republic, a Member of the European Parliament, a member of the Government, a judge of the Constitutional Court of the Slovak Republic, a judge, a prosecutor or another person holding a function in a public authority, a member of the armed forces, a person in a service relationship, a mayor, a chairman of a higher territorial unit, a member of a territorial self-government body, a state employee or an employee of a state administration body, territorial self-government or another state body, a person who exercises competence within a legal person to whom the law entrusts the power to decide in the field of public administration, a notary, a judicial executor, a member of the forest guard, water guard, fishing guard, hunting guard, nature guard or a person who has the authorisation of a member of the nature guard, if he or she participates in the fulfilment of the tasks of society and the state and uses the power that has been entrusted to him or her within the framework of responsibility for the fulfilment of these tasks. For the criminal liability and protection of a public official, the individual provisions of this Act require that the criminal offence was committed in connection with his or her power, responsibility, position or function, unless this Act provides otherwise. A public official is also a judge or official of an international judicial body recognised by the Slovak Republic or a functionary or other responsible officer of an authority active in criminal proceedings of another state, a body of the European Union or a body created jointly by the Member States of the European Union, if he or she performs acts of criminal proceedings of such state or body on the territory of the Slovak Republic; for his or her protection under the provisions of this Act it is required that he or she performs acts of criminal proceedings in accordance with an international treaty or with the consent of the authorities of the Slovak Republic. In the case of the criminal offence of acceptance and provision of undue advantage under § 336c and § 336d, a public official is also a person who, by virtue of his or her position or function, has the right to decide or participates in deciding on the disposal of property, property rights and financial resources, the management of which is subject to control by the Supreme Audit Office of the Slovak Republic. |
|---|
|  | (2) For the purposes of this Act, a foreign public official means a person |
|---|
|  | a) holding a function in a legislative body, executive body, judicial body or arbitration body, or in another public administration body of a foreign state, including the head of state, |
|---|
|  | b) holding a function, employed or working in an international organisation or supranational organisation created by states or other subjects of public international law in its body or institution, or authorised to act on their behalf, |
|---|
|  | c) holding a function, employed or working in an international judicial body, or authorised to act on its behalf, or |
|---|
|  | d) holding a function in a legal person in which a foreign state has a decisive influence, if the exercise of such function is associated with power in the conduct of public affairs and the criminal offence was committed in connection with this power or through the use of his or her position. |
|---|
|  | (3) For the purposes of this Act, a soldier means |
|---|
|  | a) a person who performs military service, |
|---|
|  | b) a member of the armed forces of the sending state for criminal offences committed on the territory of the Slovak Republic to the extent specified in an international treaty, |
|---|
|  | c) a member of the Police Force, the Prison and Court Guard Corps, the National Security Authority, the Slovak Information Service and an armed member of the financial administration, |
|---|
|  | d) a prisoner of war. |
|---|
|  | (4) Where this Act refers to military service or military duty, it means the service or duty of the persons referred to in paragraph 3(c) only in time of war or a state of war. |
|---|
|  | (5) For the purposes of this Act, a person punished for a similar act means a person who has been imposed a sanction or other measure for a similar act for a misdemeanour or other similar offence. |
|---|
|  | (6) For the purposes of this Act, a convicted person means a perpetrator who has been found guilty by a final judgment. This does not apply if the conviction has been expunged. |
|---|
|  | (7) A punished person means a convicted person who has served in whole or in part the sentence imposed by the court. This does not apply if the conviction has been expunged. |
|---|
|  | (8) If this Act requires a special quality, capacity or position of the perpetrator for the commission of a criminal offence, only a person who has the required quality, capacity or position may be a perpetrator or co-perpetrator of the criminal offence. A participant in a criminal offence may also be a person who does not have the required quality, capacity or position. If the law provides that the perpetrator must be a bearer of a special quality, capacity or position, it is sufficient if this quality, capacity or position is fulfilled by the legal person on whose behalf the perpetrator acts. |
|---|
|  | (9) Only a soldier may be a perpetrator or co-perpetrator of a military criminal offence under the eleventh chapter of the special part of this Act. |
|---|
|

| § 129 |

|---|
|  | Group of persons and organisation |
|---|
|  | (1) For the purposes of this Act, a group of persons means at least three persons. |
|---|
|  | (2) For the purposes of this Act, an organised group means an association of at least three persons for the purpose of committing a criminal offence, with a certain division of assigned tasks among the individual members of the group, the activity of which is consequently characterised by planning and coordination, which increases the probability of successful commission of the criminal offence. |
|---|
|  | (3) For the purposes of this Act, an extremist group means an association of at least three persons for the purposes of committing a criminal offence of extremism. |
|---|
|  | (4) For the purposes of this Act, a criminal group means a structured group of at least three persons which exists over a certain period of time and acts in a coordinated manner with the aim of committing one or more felonies, the criminal offence of legalisation of proceeds of crime under § 233 or any of the criminal offences of corruption under the eighth chapter of the third part of the special part for the purposes of directly or indirectly obtaining a financial or other advantage. |
|---|
|  | (5) For the purposes of this Act, a terrorist group means a group of at least three persons which exists over a certain period of time for the purposes of committing any of the criminal offences of terrorism, the activity of which is characterised by coordination; it need not have formally defined roles of its members, a stable membership base or a developed structure. |
|---|
|  | (6) Activity for a criminal group or terrorist group means intentional participation in such a group or other intentional conduct for the purpose of |
|---|
|  | a) maintaining the existence of such a group, or |
|---|
|  | b) committing the criminal offences referred to in paragraphs 4 or 5 by such a group. |
|---|
|  | (7) Support of a criminal group or terrorist group means intentional conduct consisting in the provision of financial or other means, services, cooperation or in the creation of other conditions for the purpose of |
|---|
|  | a) establishing or maintaining the existence of such a group, or |
|---|
|  | b) committing the criminal offences referred to in paragraphs 4 or 5 by such a group. |
|---|
|  | Thing |
|---|
|

| § 130 |

|---|
|  | (1) For the purposes of this Act, a thing means |
|---|
|  | a) a movable thing or immovable thing, flat or non-residential premises, animal, unless otherwise provided by individual provisions of this Act, |
|---|
|  | b) controllable natural force or energy |
|---|
|  | c) a security regardless of its form. |
|---|
|  | d) monetary funds in an account, |
|---|
|  | e) a document which is the basis for asserting a legal claim, or |
|---|
|  | f) a property right or other value assessable in money. |
|---|
|  | (2) An intangible piece of information, computer data or an image recording on a technical medium shall also be considered a thing. |
|---|
|  | (3) For the purposes of this Act, an entrusted thing means a thing owned by another person which the perpetrator has in lawful use on the basis of a contract or, for reasons of performing certain tasks, in possession at the disposal of its owner with an obligation to use it only for the agreed purpose or to return it to the owner under agreed conditions. |
|---|
|  | (4) For the purposes of this Act, appropriation of a thing means the removal of a thing from the disposition of the owner or another person who lawfully has it, without consent, with the intention to deal with it as one's own thing. |
|---|
|  | (5) For the purposes of this Act, an addictive substance means alcohol, narcotic drugs, psychotropic substances and other substances capable of adversely affecting a person's psyche or their control or recognition abilities, or social behaviour. |
|---|
|  | (6) For the purposes of this Act, a means of mass transport means a thing capable of transporting at least nine persons. |
|---|
|  | (7) For the purposes of this Act, extremist material means a written, graphic, pictorial, audio or audio-visual rendering of |
|---|
|  | a) texts and statements, flags, badges, slogans or symbols of groups and movements which aim or in the past aimed at suppressing fundamental human rights and freedoms, |
|---|
|  | b) programmes or ideologies of groups and movements which aim or in the past aimed at suppressing fundamental human rights and freedoms, |
|---|
|  | c) advocating, supporting or inciting hatred, violence or unjustifiably different treatment towards a group of persons or an individual because of their membership of a race, nation, nationality, colour, ethnic group, origin of family or because of their religious belief, if it is a pretext for the preceding reasons, or |
|---|
|  | d) justifying, approving, denying or grossly trivialising genocide, crimes against peace, crimes against humanity or war crimes, if the perpetrator or participant in this act was convicted by a final judgment of an international court established on the basis of public international law whose jurisdiction was recognised by the Slovak Republic, or by a final judgment of a court of the Slovak Republic. |
|---|
|  | (8) Material according to paragraph 7 shall not be considered extremist material if it is demonstrably produced, distributed, put into circulation, made publicly accessible or stored for the purpose of carrying out educational, collecting or research activities. |
|---|
|  | (9) For the purposes of this Act, proceeds of criminal activity means |
|---|
|  | a) a thing which was obtained by a criminal offence, |
|---|
|  | b) a thing which was obtained as a reward for a criminal offence, |
|---|
|  | c) a thing which was acquired, even if only in part, for a thing which was obtained by a criminal offence or as a reward for it, |
|---|
|  | d) a thing into which a thing which was obtained by a criminal offence or as a reward for it was converted, even if only in part, or |
|---|
|  | e) fruits and benefits of a thing referred to in letters a) to d). |
|---|
|

| § 131 |

|---|
|  | (1) For the purposes of this Act, a matter of general interest means an interest exceeding the scope of individual rights and interests of an individual which is important from the point of view of the interests of society. |
|---|
|  | (2) For the purposes of this Act, waste management shall be considered to be the collection, transport, export, import, recovery, disposal and depositing of waste. |
|---|
|  | (3) For the purposes of this Act, a bribe means a thing or other performance of a proprietary or non-proprietary nature to which there is no legal entitlement. |
|---|
|  | (4) For the purposes of this Act, an undue advantage means a performance of a proprietary or non-proprietary nature capable of influencing a public official, to which there is no legal entitlement and whose value exceeds 200 euros, or an unauthorised preferential treatment of a public official or a person close to them which is not assessable in money. An undue advantage is not a performance or preferential treatment whose acceptance is, with regard to official or materially justified interest, customary in connection with the position or function of a public official or which is recorded in the published Register of Gifts. Details shall be regulated by an implementing legal regulation. |
|---|
|  | (5) For the purposes of this Act, a public document means a written document issued on the basis of law by a state body or other public authority within its competence, which establishes, changes or terminates rights or obligations or certifies their creation, change or termination, or certifies the identity of a person or thing, their state, properties or capacity or legally protected interests. |
|---|
|  | (6) For the purposes of this Act, a payment instrument means an intangible or tangible protected device, object, record or their combination, including a payment card, which is protected against counterfeiting or unauthorised use and which enables the holder or user, alone or together with some procedure or set of procedures, to transfer money, electronic money or virtual currency. |
|---|
|  | (7) For the purposes of this Act, virtual currency means a digital bearer of value which is not issued or guaranteed by a central bank or public authority, is not necessarily linked to legal tender and which does not have the legal status of currency or money, but is accepted by some persons as an instrument of exchange which can be electronically transferred, stored or electronically traded. |
|---|
|

| § 132 |

|---|
|  | Prostitution, pornography and child pornographic performance |
|---|
|  | (1) For the purposes of this Act, prostitution means the satisfaction of sexual needs of another by sexual intercourse, another manner of sexual contact or another similar sexual contact with an adult person for remuneration. |
|---|
|  | (2) For the purposes of this Act, child prostitution means the satisfaction of sexual needs of another by sexual intercourse, another manner of sexual contact or another similar sexual contact with a child for remuneration or its promise, regardless of whether this remuneration or its promise was provided to the child or a third person. |
|---|
|  | (3) For the purposes of this Act, pornography means a depiction of sexual intercourse, another manner of sexual contact or another similar sexual contact or a depiction of exposed sexual organs intended for sexual purposes. |
|---|
|  | (4) For the purposes of this Act, child pornography means a depiction of real or simulated sexual intercourse, another manner of sexual contact or another similar sexual contact with a child or a person appearing as a child or a depiction of exposed parts of the body of a child or a person appearing as a child intended for sexual purposes. |
|---|
|  | (5) For the purposes of this Act, a child pornographic performance means a live performance intended for an audience, including with the use of information technology means, in which a child is involved in real or simulated sexual conduct or in which parts of the body of a child are exposed for sexual purposes. |
|---|
|

| § 133 |

|---|
|  | Foreign power and foreign agent |
|---|
|  | (1) For the purposes of this Act, foreign power means foreign states and their military or other groupings represented by their organisations and bodies, such as in particular persons carrying out intelligence activities, military officials, diplomats and other state officials. |
|---|
|  | (2) For the purposes of this Act, foreign agent means a natural person or legal person who, although not a body or representative of a foreign state, has significant influence in their state or in international relations by virtue of their political, economic or social position. |
|---|
|

| § 134 |

|---|
|  | Constitutional order and crisis situation |
|---|
|  | (1) For the purposes of this Act, constitutional order means the democratic system of fundamental rights and freedoms guaranteed by the proper arrangement and functioning of state authorities, territorial self-government and political parties and movements as regulated by the Constitution of the Slovak Republic. |
|---|
|  | (2) For the purposes of this Act, crisis situation means |
|---|
|  | a) state of emergency, |
|---|
|  | b) state of exception, |
|---|
|  | c) state of war, or |
|---|
|  | d) war. |
|---|
|  | (3) The provision of paragraph 2 letters a) and b) shall not apply if the criminal offence was not committed in connection with a declared state of emergency or state of exception. |
|---|
|

| § 135 |

|---|
|  | Possession of drugs for personal use |
|---|
|  | (1) Possession of a narcotic drug, psychotropic substance, poison or precursor for personal use means to unlawfully have in one's possession for any period of time a narcotic drug, psychotropic substance, poison or precursor in a quantity corresponding to at most three times the usual single dose for use, for personal consumption. |
|---|
|  | (2) Possession of a narcotic drug, psychotropic substance, poison or precursor for personal use on a larger scale means to unlawfully have in one's possession for any period of time a narcotic drug, psychotropic substance, poison or precursor in a quantity corresponding to at most ten times the usual single dose for use, for personal consumption. |
|---|
|

| § 135a |

|---|
|  | Possession of substances with anabolic or other hormonal effect |
|---|
|  | (1) Possession of substances with anabolic or other hormonal effect in a larger quantity means to unlawfully have in one's possession for any period of time a substance with anabolic or other hormonal effect pursuant to a special regulation in a quantity corresponding to at least ten times one usual indivisible form, in particular one tablet, one injection, one ampoule, or similar indivisible packaging of a preparation containing substances with anabolic or other hormonal effect. |
|---|
|  | (2) Possession of substances with anabolic or other hormonal effect in a substantial quantity means to unlawfully have in one's possession for any period of time a substance with anabolic or other hormonal effect pursuant to a special regulation in a quantity corresponding to at least one hundred times one usual indivisible form, in particular one tablet, one injection, one ampoule, or similar indivisible packaging of a preparation containing substances with anabolic or other hormonal effect. |
|---|
|  | (3) Possession of substances with anabolic or other hormonal effect in a large quantity means to unlawfully have in one's possession for any period of time a substance with anabolic or other hormonal effect pursuant to a special regulation in a quantity corresponding to at least one thousand times one usual indivisible form, in particular one tablet, one injection, one ampoule, or similar indivisible packaging of a preparation containing substances with anabolic or other hormonal effect. |
|---|
|

| § 136 |

|---|
|  | Calculation of time |
|---|
|  | Where this Act associates any effect with the expiry of a certain period calculated in days, the day on which the legal fact determining its commencement occurred shall not be included in it. |
|---|
|

| § 137 |

|---|
|  | Unauthorised absence |
|---|
|  | (1) A person absents themselves unauthorisedly who |
|---|
|  | a) absents themselves without permission from their unit or from the place of service assignment, |
|---|
|  | b) fails to report to their unit or to the place of service assignment, although they were sent there or although the time of their justified absence has expired, in particular upon appointment, transfer, official trip or leave, |
|---|
|  | c) was separated during combat operations from their unit and did not join their own or another military unit after the cessation of the cause of separation, or |
|---|
|  | d) failed to report to any military unit after return or after release from enemy captivity. |
|---|
|  | (2) A person absents themselves unauthorisedly repeatedly who absents themselves unauthorisedly for a period even shorter than forty-eight hours at least three times, if a period of more than three months has not elapsed between the first and third unauthorised absence. |
|---|
|  | (3) Unauthorised absence pursuant to paragraph 1 applies only to a person performing compulsory military service. |
|---|
|  | Part Two |
|---|
|  | Special qualifying terms |
|---|
|

| § 138 |

|---|
|  | More serious manner of conduct |
|---|
|  | More serious manner of conduct means the commission of a criminal offence |
|---|
|  | a) with a weapon, except for the criminal offences of premeditated murder pursuant to § 144, murder pursuant to § 145, manslaughter pursuant to § 147 and § 148, killing pursuant to § 149, bodily harm pursuant to § 155, § 156 and § 157, |
|---|
|  | b) for a longer time, |
|---|
|  | c) in a cruel or tormenting manner, |
|---|
|  | d) by violence, threat of imminent violence or threat of other serious harm, |
|---|
|  | e) by breaking in, |
|---|
|  | f) by deceit, |
|---|
|  | g) by exploiting distress, inexperience, dependence or subordination, |
|---|
|  | h) by breaching an important duty arising from the perpetrator's employment, position or function or imposed on them by law, |
|---|
|  | i) by an organised group, or |
|---|
|  | j) against several persons. |
|---|
|

| § 139 |

|---|
|  | Protected person |
|---|
|  | (1) Protected person means |
|---|
|  | a) a child, |
|---|
|  | b) a pregnant woman, |
|---|
|  | c) a close person, |
|---|
|  | d) a dependent person, |
|---|
|  | e) an elderly person, |
|---|
|  | f) a sick person, |
|---|
|  | g) a person enjoying protection under international law, |
|---|
|  | h) a public official or a person fulfilling their duties imposed on the basis of law, |
|---|
|  | i) a witness, expert, interpreter or translator, or |
|---|
|  | j) a health care worker in the performance of a health care profession aimed at saving life or protecting health. |
|---|
|  | (2) The provision of paragraph 1 shall not apply if the criminal offence was not committed in connection with the position, status or age of the protected person. |
|---|
|

| § 140 |

|---|
|  | Special motive |
|---|
|  | Special motive means the commission of a criminal offence |
|---|
|  | a) on commission, |
|---|
|  | b) out of revenge, |
|---|
|  | c) with the intention to conceal or facilitate another criminal offence, |
|---|
|  | d) with the intention to commit one of the criminal offences of terrorism, |
|---|
|  | e) out of hatred towards a group of persons or an individual because of their actual or presumed affiliation to a race, nation, nationality, ethnic group, because of their actual or presumed origin, colour, sex, sexual orientation, political conviction or religious belief, or |
|---|
|  | f) with a sexual motive. |
|---|
|

| § 140a |

|---|
|  | Crimes of Extremism |
|---|
|  | Crimes of extremism are the crime of establishment, support and promotion of a movement aimed at suppressing fundamental rights and freedoms under § 421, expression of sympathy for a movement aimed at suppressing fundamental rights and freedoms under § 422, production of extremist material under § 422a, dissemination of extremist material under § 422b, possession of extremist material under § 422c, denial and approval of the holocaust, crimes of political regimes and crimes against humanity under § 422d, defamation of nation, race and belief under § 423, incitement to national, racial and ethnic hatred under § 424, apartheid and discrimination of a group of persons under § 424a and a crime committed from a special motive under § 140 letter e). |
|---|
|

| § 140b |

|---|
|  | Crimes of Terrorism |
|---|
|  | Crimes of terrorism are the crime of establishment, conspiracy and support of a terrorist group under § 297, the crime of terror under § 313 and 314, the crime of terrorist attack under § 419, the crime of certain forms of participation in terrorism under § 419b, the crime of financing of terrorism under § 419c, the crime of travel for the purpose of terrorism under § 419d and a felony committed from a special motive under § 140 letter d). |
|---|
|

| § 141 |

|---|
|  | Dangerous Association |
|---|
|  | A dangerous association means |
|---|
|  | a) a criminal group, or |
|---|
|  | b) a terrorist group. |
|---|
|

| § 142 |

|---|
|  | Serious Bodily Harm or Death |
|---|
|  | (1) Serious bodily harm means causing bodily harm referred to in § 123 paragraph 3. |
|---|
|  | (2) Death means biological death of the brain (cerebral death). |
|---|
|

| § 143 |

|---|
|  | Death of Multiple Persons |
|---|
|  | Death of multiple persons means causing the death of at least three persons. |
|---|
|  | PART TWO |
|---|
|  | SPECIAL PART |
|---|
|  | FIRST TITLE |
|---|
|  | CRIMES AGAINST LIFE AND HEALTH |
|---|
|  | First Section |
|---|
|  | Crimes Against Life |
|---|
|

| § 144 |

|---|
|  | Premeditated Murder |
|---|
|  | (1) Whoever intentionally kills another with premeditated motive shall be punished by imprisonment for twenty to twenty-five years. |
|---|
|  | (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for twenty-five years or by life imprisonment if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 |
|---|
|  | a) and has already been convicted of the crime of murder, |
|---|
|  | b) on two persons, |
|---|
|  | c) by a more serious manner of conduct, |
|---|
|  | d) on a protected person, |
|---|
|  | e) from a special motive, or |
|---|
|  | f) with the intention to obtain pecuniary gain. |
|---|
|  | (3) The offender shall be punished by life imprisonment if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 |
|---|
|  | a) and has already been convicted of such act, |
|---|
|  | b) in a dangerous association, or |
|---|
|  | c) during a crisis situation. |
|---|
|

| § 145 |

|---|
|  | Murder |
|---|
|  | (1) Whoever intentionally kills another shall be punished by imprisonment for fifteen to twenty years. |
|---|
|  | (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for twenty to twenty-five years or by life imprisonment if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 |
|---|
|  | a) on two persons, |
|---|
|  | b) by a more serious manner of conduct, |
|---|
|  | c) on a protected person, |
|---|
|  | d) from a special motive, or |
|---|
|  | e) with the intention to obtain pecuniary gain. |
|---|
|  | (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for twenty-five years or by life imprisonment if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 |
|---|
|  | a) and has already been convicted of such act or of the crime of premeditated murder, |
|---|
|  | b) in a dangerous association, or |
|---|
|  | c) during a crisis situation. |
|---|
|

| § 146 |

|---|
|  | Murder of a Newborn Child by the Mother |
|---|
|  | A mother who, in a state of disturbance caused by childbirth, intentionally kills her newborn child during childbirth or immediately thereafter shall be punished by imprisonment for four to eight years. |
|---|
|  | Manslaughter |
|---|
|

| § 147 |

|---|
|  | (1) Whoever with the intention to cause serious bodily harm to another negligently causes death shall be punished by imprisonment for seven to ten years. |
|---|
|  | (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for nine to twelve years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 |
|---|
|  | a) on a protected person, or |
|---|
|  | b) from a special motive. |
|---|
|  | (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for twelve to fifteen years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 |
|---|
|  | a) by a more serious manner of conduct, |
|---|
|  | b) as a member of a dangerous association, or |
|---|
|  | c) during a crisis situation. |
|---|
|

| § 148 |

|---|
|  | (1) Whoever with the intention to injure the health of another negligently causes death shall be punished by imprisonment for three to eight years. |
|---|
|  | (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for five to ten years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 |
|---|
|  | a) on a protected person, or |
|---|
|  | b) from a special motive. |
|---|
|  | (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for seven to twelve years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 |
|---|
|  | a) by a more serious manner of conduct, |
|---|
|  | b) as a member of a dangerous association, or |
|---|
|  | c) during a crisis situation. |
|---|
|

| § 149 |

|---|
|  | Causing Death |
|---|
|  | (1) Whoever negligently causes the death of another shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years. |
|---|
|  | (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two to five years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 |
|---|
|  | a) by a more serious manner of conduct, or |
|---|
|  | b) on a protected person. |
|---|
|  | (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for four to ten years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 on two or more persons because he grossly violated regulations on occupational safety or traffic safety or hygiene regulations. |
|---|
|  | (4) The offender shall be punished in the same manner as in paragraph 3 if, in connection with his employment, occupation, position or function or as a driver of a means of transport, he causes the negligent death of another in a state precluding the capacity to perform such activity, which he induced by the influence of an addictive substance. |
|---|
|  | (5) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for seven to twelve years if by the act referred to in paragraph 4 he negligently causes the death of two or more persons. |
|---|
|  | Unlawful Termination of Pregnancy |
|---|
|

| § 150 |

|---|
|  | (1) Whoever artificially terminates the pregnancy of a pregnant woman without her consent shall be punished by imprisonment for three to eight years. |
|---|
|  | (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for four to ten years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 |
|---|
|  | a) by a more serious manner of conduct, or |
|---|
|  | b) on a protected person. |
|---|
|  | (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for eight to fifteen years if by the act referred to in paragraph 1 he causes serious bodily harm or death to the pregnant woman. |
|---|
|

| § 151 |

|---|
|  | (1) Whoever with the consent of a pregnant woman artificially terminates her pregnancy by a procedure or under conditions that are contrary to generally binding legal regulations on artificial termination of pregnancy shall be punished by imprisonment for two to five years. |
|---|
|  | (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to eight years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 |
|---|
|  | a) and thereby causes serious bodily harm or death, |
|---|
|  | b) on a minor woman without the consent of her legal representative or the person to whom she was entrusted for care or supervision, |
|---|
|  | c) and thereby obtains greater gain, or |
|---|
|  | d) by a more serious manner of conduct. |
|---|
|

| § 152 |

|---|
|  | (1) Whoever induces a pregnant woman to |
|---|
|  | a) artificially terminate her pregnancy herself, or |
|---|
|  | b) request another person or allow another person to artificially terminate her pregnancy by a procedure or under conditions that are contrary to generally binding legal regulations on artificial termination of pregnancy, |
|---|
|  | shall be punished by imprisonment for up to one year. |
|---|
|  | (2) The same punishment as in paragraph 1 shall be imposed on whoever assists a pregnant woman in terminating her pregnancy by herself or in requesting another person or allowing another person to artificially terminate her pregnancy. |
|---|
|  | (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two to five years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2 |
|---|
|  | a) by a more serious manner of conduct, or |
|---|
|  | b) against a protected person. |
|---|
|  | (4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to eight years if by the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2 he causes serious harm to health or death. |
|---|
|

| § 153 |

|---|
|  | A pregnant woman who artificially terminates her pregnancy herself or requests another person to do so, or allows it, shall not be criminally liable for such an act, not even under the provisions on instigators and accomplices. |
|---|
|

| § 154 |

|---|
|  | Participation in suicide |
|---|
|  | (1) Whoever incites another person to commit suicide or assists another person in committing suicide, if at least an attempt at suicide has occurred, shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to three years. |
|---|
|  | (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to eight years if the act referred to in paragraph 1 |
|---|
|  | a) is committed by a more serious manner of conduct, |
|---|
|  | b) against a protected person, or |
|---|
|  | c) from a special motive. |
|---|
|  | Part Two |
|---|
|  | Criminal offences against health |
|---|
|  | Bodily harm |
|---|
|

| § 155 |

|---|
|  | (1) Whoever intentionally causes serious harm to the health of another person shall be punished by imprisonment for four to ten years. |
|---|
|  | (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for five to twelve years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 |
|---|
|  | a) by a more serious manner of conduct, |
|---|
|  | b) against a protected person, or |
|---|
|  | c) from a special motive. |
|---|
|  | (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for ten to fifteen years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 |
|---|
|  | a) as a member of a dangerous grouping, or |
|---|
|  | b) during a crisis situation. |
|---|
|

| § 156 |

|---|
|  | (1) Whoever intentionally harms the health of another person shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to two years. |
|---|
|  | (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for one to three years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 |
|---|
|  | a) against a protected person, or |
|---|
|  | b) from a special motive. |
|---|
|  | (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two to five years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 |
|---|
|  | a) by a more serious manner of conduct, |
|---|
|  | b) during a crisis situation, or |
|---|
|  | c) and thereby causes serious harm to health. |
|---|
|

| § 157 |

|---|
|  | (1) Whoever negligently causes serious harm to the health of another person shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to two years. |
|---|
|  | (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for one to five years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 |
|---|
|  | a) by a more serious manner of conduct, or |
|---|
|  | b) against a protected person. |
|---|
|

| § 158 |

|---|
|  | Whoever negligently harms the health of another person by breaching an important duty arising from his employment, occupation, position or function or imposed on him by law shall be punished by imprisonment for up to one year. |
|---|
|  | Unauthorised removal of organs, tissues and cells and unlawful sterilisation |
|---|
|

| § 159 |

|---|
|  | (1) Whoever unlawfully removes an organ, tissue or cell from a living person or whoever unlawfully procures such organ, tissue or cell for himself or another person shall be punished by imprisonment for two to eight years. |
|---|
|  | (2) The same punishment as in paragraph 1 shall be imposed on whoever unlawfully sterilises a natural person. |
|---|
|  | (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for seven to twelve years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2 |
|---|
|  | a) by a more serious manner of conduct, |
|---|
|  | b) from a special motive, or |
|---|
|  | c) as a member of a dangerous grouping. |
|---|
|  | (4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for ten to fifteen years if by the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2 he causes serious harm to health or death. |
|---|
|

| § 160 |

|---|
|  | (1) Whoever unlawfully procures for himself or another person an organ, tissue or cell from a deceased person shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to three years. |
|---|
|  | (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for one to five years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 |
|---|
|  | a) by a more serious manner of conduct, or |
|---|
|  | b) from a special motive. |
|---|
|  | (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to eight years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 as a member of a dangerous grouping. |
|---|
|  | Part Three |
|---|
|  | Criminal offences endangering life or health |
|---|
|

| § 161 |

|---|
|  | Unauthorised experiment on a human being and cloning of a human being |
|---|
|  | (1) Whoever, under the pretext of obtaining new medical knowledge, methods or to confirm hypotheses, or for clinical testing of medicines, carries out verification of new medical knowledge without authorisation |
|---|
|  | a) despite the fact that it directly endangers the life or health of a human being, unless it concerns necessary procedures capable of saving his directly endangered life, or |
|---|
|  | b) without medical indication and without the consent of the person concerned, or carries it out on persons on whom verification without medical indication is prohibited, or carries it out on a human foetus or embryo, or carries it out in breach of other legal conditions for verification without medical indication, |
|---|
|  | shall be punished by imprisonment for one to five years. |
|---|
|  | (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to eight years if he carries out any intervention with the aim of creating a human being at any stage of its development genetically identical to another human being, whether living or dead. |
|---|
|  | (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for five to twelve years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2 |
|---|
|  | a) by a more serious manner of conduct, |
|---|
|  | b) against a protected person, or |
|---|
|  | c) if by the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2 he obtains greater benefit for himself or another person. |
|---|
|  | (4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for eight to fifteen years if by the act referred to in paragraph 1 he causes serious harm to health or death. |
|---|
|

| § 162 |

|---|
|  | Damage to health |
|---|
|  | (1) Whoever, despite not having the prescribed professional competence of a health care worker or other professional worker in health care, carries out acts of health care and by an unprofessional examination or treatment procedure or by incorrect indication of medicines, medicinal products or other medical devices, even negligently, |
|---|
|  | a) directly endangers the life of another person, |
|---|
|  | b) harms the health of another person, or |
|---|
|  | c) carries out an examination or treatment procedure or incorrect indication of medicines, medicinal products or other medical devices without the consent of another person and by such procedure endangers his health, |
|---|
|  | shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to three years. |
|---|
|  | (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for one to five years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 |
|---|
|  | a) by a more serious manner of conduct, or |
|---|
|  | b) against a protected person. |
|---|
|  | (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to eight years if by the act referred to in paragraph 1 he causes serious harm to health or death. |
|---|
|  | Spreading of a dangerous contagious human disease |
|---|
|

| § 163 |

|---|
|  | (1) Whoever intentionally causes or increases the danger of introduction or spread of a dangerous contagious human disease shall be punished by imprisonment for one to five years. |
|---|
|  | (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to eight years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 in a more serious manner of commission. |
|---|
|  | (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for four to ten years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 |
|---|
|  | a) and thereby causes serious bodily harm or death, or |
|---|
|  | b) during a crisis situation. |
|---|
|

| § 164 |

|---|
|  | (1) Whoever negligently causes or increases the danger of introduction or spread of a contagious human disease shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years. |
|---|
|  | (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for one to five years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 |
|---|
|  | a) in a more serious manner of commission, or |
|---|
|  | b) and thereby causes serious bodily harm or death. |
|---|
|  | Endangerment by Human Immunodeficiency Virus |
|---|
|

| § 165 |

|---|
|  | (1) Whoever intentionally exposes another to the danger of infection with the human immunodeficiency virus shall be punished by imprisonment for three to ten years. |
|---|
|  | (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for seven to twelve years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 |
|---|
|  | a) in a more serious manner of commission, or |
|---|
|  | b) on a protected person. |
|---|
|  | (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for ten to fifteen years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 and thereby causes serious bodily harm or death. |
|---|
|

| § 166 |

|---|
|  | (1) Whoever negligently exposes another to the danger of infection with the human immunodeficiency virus shall be punished by imprisonment for one to five years. |
|---|
|  | (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to eight years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 |
|---|
|  | a) in a more serious manner of commission, or |
|---|
|  | b) on a protected person. |
|---|
|  | (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for four to ten years if by the act referred to in paragraph 1 he causes serious bodily harm or death. |
|---|
|

| § 167 |

|---|
|  | Endangerment by Venereal Disease |
|---|
|  | (1) Whoever exposes another, even negligently, to the danger of venereal infection shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years. |
|---|
|  | (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for one to five years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 on a protected person. |
|---|
|  | (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to eight years if by the act referred to in paragraph 1 he causes serious bodily harm or death. |
|---|
|  | Endangerment of Health by Defective Foodstuffs and Other Items |
|---|
|

| § 168 |

|---|
|  | (1) Whoever has for sale, produces or intentionally procures for himself or another defective foodstuffs or other items whose consumption or use for the usual purpose is dangerous to human health shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years. |
|---|
|  | (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for one to five years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 in a more serious manner of commission. |
|---|
|  | (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to eight years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 |
|---|
|  | a) and thereby causes serious bodily harm or death, or |
|---|
|  | b) during a crisis situation. |
|---|
|

| § 169 |

|---|
|  | (1) Whoever has for sale or for such purpose produces, or negligently procures for himself or another foodstuffs or other items whose consumption or use for the usual purpose is dangerous to human health shall be punished by imprisonment for up to one year. |
|---|
|  | (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to three years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 in a more serious manner of commission. |
|---|
|  | (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two to five years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 |
|---|
|  | a) and thereby causes serious bodily harm or death, or |
|---|
|  | b) during a crisis situation. |
|---|
|

| § 170 |

|---|
|  | Endangerment of Health by Unauthorised Medicines, Medicinal Products and Medical Devices |
|---|
|  | (1) Whoever, even negligently, causes or increases the danger of endangerment of human health by |
|---|
|  | a) unauthorisedly handling medicines, medicinal products or medical devices, or |
|---|
|  | b) conducting clinical trials of medicines, medicinal products or medical devices in breach of a generally binding legal regulation, |
|---|
|  | shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years. |
|---|
|  | (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for one to five years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 |
|---|
|  | a) in the provision of health care, |
|---|
|  | b) in a more serious manner of commission, |
|---|
|  | c) on a protected person, or |
|---|
|  | d) from a special motive. |
|---|
|  | (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to eight years if by the act referred to in paragraph 1 he causes serious bodily harm or death. |
|---|
|

| § 170a |

|---|
|  | Unauthorised Handling of Medicines, Medicinal Products and Medical Devices |
|---|
|  | (1) Whoever unauthorisedly on a larger scale |
|---|
|  | a) produces, |
|---|
|  | b) imports, exports, transports or has transported, or |
|---|
|  | c) purchases, sells, exchanges, stores or procures |
|---|
|  | medicines or medicinal products that are not authorised under a special regulation, or medical devices that have been placed on the market in breach of a generally binding legal regulation, or whoever intermediates such activity, shall be punished by imprisonment for one to five years. |
|---|
|  | (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to eight years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 |
|---|
|  | a) although he was convicted of such an act in the preceding twelve months or was sanctioned for a similar act in the preceding twelve months, |
|---|
|  | b) in a more serious manner of commission, |
|---|
|  | c) on a protected person, |
|---|
|  | d) publicly, or |
|---|
|  | e) on a considerable scale. |
|---|
|  | (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for five to twelve years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 |
|---|
|  | a) and thereby causes serious bodily harm or death, or |
|---|
|  | b) on a large scale. |
|---|
|  | (4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for ten to fifteen years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 |
|---|
|  | a) and thereby causes serious bodily harm to several persons or death of several persons, or |
|---|
|  | b) as a member of a dangerous grouping. |
|---|
|

| § 170b |

|---|
|  | Counterfeiting of Medicines and Medical Devices |
|---|
|  | (1) Whoever procures for himself or another counterfeit medicines or a counterfeit medical device or whoever possesses, imports, exports, transports, offers or sells such medicines or medical device shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years. |
|---|
|  | (2) Imprisonment for one year to five years shall be imposed on whoever counterfeits or otherwise alters the identity |
|---|
|  | a) of a medicine including its labelling or provides false information about the manufacturer, the holder of the marketing authorisation for the medicine or about the country of manufacture with the intention of passing them off as genuine, |
|---|
|  | b) of a medical device including its labelling or provides false information about the manufacturer, about conformity assessment, about the conformity mark, about notification of the medical device or about the country of manufacture with the intention of passing it off as genuine. |
|---|
|  | (3) Imprisonment for three years to eight years shall be imposed on the offender if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2 |
|---|
|  | a) although he was convicted of such an act in the preceding twelve months or was punished for a similar act in the preceding twelve months, |
|---|
|  | b) by a more serious manner of commission, |
|---|
|  | c) against a protected person, |
|---|
|  | d) publicly, or |
|---|
|  | e) on a larger scale. |
|---|
|  | (4) Imprisonment for five years to twelve years shall be imposed on the offender if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2 |
|---|
|  | a) and thereby causes serious bodily harm or death, or |
|---|
|  | b) on a considerable scale. |
|---|
|  | (5) Imprisonment for ten years to fifteen years shall be imposed on the offender if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2 |
|---|
|  | a) and thereby causes serious bodily harm to several persons or the death of several persons, |
|---|
|  | b) as a member of a dangerous grouping, or |
|---|
|  | c) on a large scale. |
|---|
|  | Unauthorised Production of Narcotic Drugs and Psychotropic Substances, Poisons or Precursors, their Possession and Trading in them |
|---|
|

| § 171 |

|---|
|  | (1) Whoever unlawfully possesses a narcotic drug or psychotropic substance from a plant of the genus cannabis for his own use shall be punished by imprisonment for up to one year. |
|---|
|  | (2) Whoever unlawfully possesses a narcotic drug or psychotropic substance referred to in paragraph 1 for his own use on a larger scale shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years. |
|---|
|  | (3) Whoever unlawfully possesses a narcotic drug or psychotropic substance other than that referred to in paragraph 1, a poison or a precursor for his own use shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years. |
|---|
|  | (4) Whoever unlawfully possesses a narcotic drug or psychotropic substance other than that referred to in paragraph 1, a poison or a precursor for his own use on a larger scale shall be punished by imprisonment for up to five years. |
|---|
|

| § 172 |

|---|
|  | (1) Whoever unlawfully possesses for any period of time a narcotic drug or psychotropic substance from a plant of the genus cannabis shall be punished by imprisonment for three years to seven years. |
|---|
|  | (2) Imprisonment for five years to ten years shall be imposed on the offender if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 and has already been convicted of such an act. |
|---|
|  | (3) Whoever unlawfully possesses for any period of time a narcotic drug or psychotropic substance other than that referred to in paragraph 1, a poison or a precursor shall be punished by imprisonment for three years to ten years. |
|---|
|  | (4) Whoever unlawfully |
|---|
|  | a) produces, |
|---|
|  | b) imports, exports, transports or has transported, or |
|---|
|  | c) purchases, sells, exchanges, procures, |
|---|
|  | a narcotic drug, psychotropic substance, poison or precursor or whoever intermediates such activity shall be punished by imprisonment for three years to ten years. |
|---|
|  | (5) Imprisonment for ten years to fifteen years shall be imposed on the offender if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 3 or 4 and has already been convicted of such an act. |
|---|
|  | (6) Imprisonment for ten years to fifteen years shall be imposed on the offender if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1, 3 or 4 |
|---|
|  | a) for a person who is being treated for drug addiction, |
|---|
|  | b) by a more serious manner of commission, |
|---|
|  | c) against a protected person, or |
|---|
|  | d) on a larger scale. |
|---|
|  | (7) Imprisonment for fifteen years to twenty years shall be imposed on the offender if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1, 3 or 4 |
|---|
|  | a) and thereby causes serious bodily harm or death, |
|---|
|  | b) against a person younger than fifteen years or through such a person, or |
|---|
|  | c) on a considerable scale. |
|---|
|  | (8) Imprisonment for twenty years to twenty-five years or a sentence of life imprisonment shall be imposed on the offender if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1, 3 or 4 |
|---|
|  | a) and thereby causes serious bodily harm to several persons or the death of several persons, |
|---|
|  | b) as a member of a dangerous grouping, or |
|---|
|  | c) on a large scale. |
|---|
|

| § 173 |

|---|
|  | (1) Whoever produces, procures for himself or another or possesses an object intended for the unauthorised production of a narcotic drug, psychotropic substance, poison and precursor shall be punished by imprisonment for up to five years. |
|---|
